# Luma AMD Speedrun: Phase 12 — hiprtc + Local Model Delegation

## Context

Phases 1–11 exhausted all Python-level API paths. The bottleneck is **Python dispatch
overhead (~130µs)** — the leader at 4.3µs MUST bypass Python entirely.

| Kernel | Our Time | Leader | Gap | Bottleneck |
|--------|----------|--------|-----|------------|
| MLA | ~70 µs | 4.3 µs | 16x | Python dispatch + aiter pipeline |
| GEMM | ~23 µs | 9.7 µs | 2.4x | Two-kernel pipeline (quant + GEMM) |
| MoE | ~155 µs | 145 µs | 1.07x | Near API ceiling |

**Token budget constraint:** ~50% of weekly Claude Code budget used in 2 days.
Claude must act as **architect + reviewer + submitter only**. All heavy-lifting
(kernel drafting, iteration, debugging) delegated to local finetuned models.

## Architecture: Claude as Orchestrator

```
Claude Code (architect)
  │
  ├─ Designs kernel specs (HIP C++ structure, MFMA patterns, memory layout)
  ├─ Writes structured prompts for local model
  ├─ Reviews local model output (correctness check)
  ├─ Submits via popcorn-cli
  │
  ▼
Local Finetuned Model (kernel engineer) — runs on Strix Halo (128GB RAM)
  │
  ├─ Drafts HIP C++ kernel source from spec
  ├─ Iterates on correctness (compile errors, numeric issues)
  ├─ Generates submission.py variants
  └─ Runs via Ollama API or cohezion compound system
```

**Token savings:** ~10x reduction in Claude usage. Claude writes ~500 tokens of spec,
local model generates ~2000 tokens of kernel code. Claude reviews ~200 tokens.
vs current: Claude generates entire kernel (~3000+ tokens per iteration).

## Phase 0: Build Local Kernel-Writing Model (LOCAL ONLY — no Claude tokens)

### 0A: Collect Training Data

Gather HIP/CUDA kernel source code for finetuning:

```bash
# 1. HipKittens source (state-of-art AMD kernels, ~500 LOC attention kernels)
git clone https://github.com/HazyResearch/HipKittens /tmp/hipkittens-data

# 2. aiter source (AMD's production MLA/GEMM/MoE kernels)
# Already on runner at /opt/rocm or pip show aiter
pip show aiter | grep Location  # find source
cp -r $(pip show aiter | grep Location | cut -d' ' -f2)/aiter /tmp/aiter-data

# 3. AMD ROCm examples (runtime compilation, MFMA usage)
git clone https://github.com/ROCm/rocm-examples /tmp/rocm-examples

# 4. Our own submission variants (11 phases of kernel code)
cp -r research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/ /tmp/our-kernels

# 5. AMD CDNA4 matrix core programming guide
# Fetch from: https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/matrix-cores-cdna/
# Save as markdown for training context

# 6. hip-python examples
# From: https://rocm.docs.amd.com/projects/hip-python/en/latest/user_guide/1_usage.html
```

### 0B: Finetune Local Model

**Base model:** `qwen3-coder:30b` (best code model that fits in 128GB unified RAM)
**Method:** LoRA finetune on collected HIP/CUDA kernel corpus
**Focus areas:**
- HIP C++ kernel syntax (`__global__`, `__shared__`, `blockIdx`, `threadIdx`)
- MFMA intrinsics (`__builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_16x16x16_bf16`)
- hiprtc compilation patterns (string→compile→load→launch)
- Online softmax for attention
- Memory coalescing and LDS usage on CDNA4
- MLA decode attention structure (Q@K^T→softmax→@V with MQA)

**Tooling:** Use Unsloth or axolotl for LoRA finetune, export to GGUF for Ollama.

```bash
# Example training script structure (run locally, zero Claude tokens)
python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py \
  --base-model qwen3-coder:30b \
  --data-dir /tmp/kernel-training-data \
  --output-dir /tmp/kernel-model-lora \
  --method qlora \
  --epochs 3 \
  --lr 2e-4
```

### 0C: Deploy to Ollama

```bash
# Convert LoRA to GGUF and register with Ollama
ollama create hip-kernel-dev -f Modelfile
# Modelfile points to the finetuned GGUF
```

### 0D: Build Kernel-Writing PRIME Skill

Create `src/cohezion/skills/HIP_KERNEL_ENGINEER_PRIME.md`:

```markdown
# HIP Kernel Engineer

## Objective
Generate HIP C++ GPU kernels for AMD MI355X (gfx950, CDNA4) that compile
via hiprtc and launch via hipModuleLaunchKernel from Python.

## Input
- Kernel specification (workload, data layout, grid/block config)
- Target architecture details (CU count, memory bandwidth, MFMA specs)
- Correctness requirements (reference output, tolerances)

## Output
- Complete HIP C++ kernel source as a Python string constant
- submission.py wrapper with compile/load/launch logic
- Expected performance analysis

## Constraints
- Must compile with hiprtcCompileProgram --gpu-architecture=gfx950
- All code in a single submission.py file
- Fallback to ref_kernel on any error
```

## Phase A: Environment Probe (Claude: ~200 tokens)

**Claude writes:** A probe submission (~50 lines) that tests hip-python availability.
**Submit via:** popcorn-cli `--mode test`

Probe for:
1. `from hip import hip, hiprtc`
2. `ctypes.CDLL("libhiprtc.so")`
3. `hipcc --version`
4. `torch.cuda.get_arch_list()`

**Decision gate:**
- hip available → proceed to Phase B
- Not available → fall back to parameter tuning only

## Phase B: Validate hiprtc Pipeline (Claude: ~300 tokens + local model)

**Claude writes:** Kernel spec for simple vector copy
**Local model generates:** HIP C++ source + submission.py wrapper
**Claude reviews:** Correctness of compile/launch code, submits

**B1:** Vector copy — validate string→compile→load→launch→verify
**B2:** BF16 dot product — validate MFMA intrinsics + torch tensor passing

## Phase C: MLA Decode Kernel (Claude: ~500 tokens + local model iterations)

**Claude writes:** Detailed MLA decode spec:
```
Workload: Q[total_q, 16, 576] @ KV[total_kv, 1, 576]^T → softmax → @V[:,:512]
MQA: 16 Q heads, 1 KV head. qseqlen=1 (decode). sm_scale=1/sqrt(576).
Grid: (bs, nheads). Block: 256. Online softmax. Fused score+value accumulation.
Target: <15µs geomean across 8 benchmark shapes.
```

**Local model generates:** Full HIP C++ MLA kernel (~200-300 lines)
**Claude reviews:** Checks correctness patterns, submits, analyzes results
**Local model iterates:** Fix compile errors, numeric issues based on test output

Refinement ladder:
1. C1: bf16 naive (correctness first)
2. C2: bf16 + online softmax (single pass)
3. C3: fp8 KV (half bandwidth)
4. C4: MFMA optimized (peak compute)
5. C5: chiplet-aware scheduling (XCD-aware grid)

## Phase D: Fused GEMM (if time permits)

Same delegation pattern: Claude specs → local model drafts → Claude reviews + submits.
Fuses `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `gemm_a4w4` into single hiprtc kernel.

## MI355X Architecture Reference (for kernel specs)

- 256 CUs × 8 XCDs, 9.2 PFLOPS FP4, 4.6 PFLOPS FP8, 2.3 PFLOPS BF16
- 288GB HBM3E @ 8 TB/s, MFMA with block exponent scaling
- 256 VGPRs + 256 AGPRs per SIMD, 64KB LDS per CU
- Key MFMA: `__builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_16x16x16_bf16` (bf16 outer product)
- Key MFMA: `__builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_32x32x64_fp4` (native FP4, gfx950 only)
- [HipKittens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08083): 99% peak, 3x Triton
- [CDNA4 matrix cores](https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/matrix-cores-cdna/README.html)
- [hip-python](https://rocm.docs.amd.com/projects/hip-python/en/latest/user_guide/1_usage.html)
- [AITER MLA tutorial](https://rocm.docs.amd.com/projects/ai-developer-hub/en/latest/notebooks/gpu_dev_optimize/aiter_mla_decode_kernel.html)

## Execution Order

```
Phase 0: Build local kernel model ── (LOCAL ONLY, no Claude tokens)
  ↓
Phase A: Probe runner ───────────── (Claude: ~200 tokens, 1 submit)
  ↓ (gate: hip available?)
Phase B: Validate pipeline ──────── (Claude: ~300 tokens, 2 submits)
  ↓ (gate: dispatch < 10µs?)
Phase C: MLA kernel iterations ──── (Claude: ~500 tokens per iter, local model: bulk)
  ↓ (leaderboard if improved)
Phase D: Fused GEMM ─────────────── (same pattern, if time permits)
```

**Estimated Claude token spend:** ~2000 tokens total for Phases A-C
(vs ~15000+ tokens if Claude writes all kernel code directly)

# Luma AMD Speedrun: Phase 9 — Team-Parallel Kernel Optimization

## Context

**Phase 8 is complete.** Three kernels are on the leaderboard. Significant gaps remain.

| Kernel | Our Time | Leader | Gap | Current Approach | Bottleneck |
|--------|----------|--------|-----|-----------------|------------|
| MLA | ~129 µs | 4.3 µs | **30x** | FlashDecoding + hybrid routing (threshold=100k) | VGPR pressure (kv1[64,512]=256 VGPRs); bf16 bandwidth |
| GEMM | ~20.6 µs | 9.7 µs | **2.1x** | `gemm_afp4wfp4` (Triton) | API ceiling; gemm_a4w4 blocked by callsite sensitivity |
| MoE | ~155 µs | 145 µs | **1.07x** | Adaptive KSPLIT routing | API ceiling |

**Phase 8 key learnings** (skills: `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla`, `amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness`):
1. VGPR pressure from KV tile, not accumulator — BLOCK_M tiling didn't help
2. BLOCK_M tiling increases KV bandwidth (nq/BLOCK_M independent KV loads)
3. ref_kernel has ~130 µs fixed overhead (metadata + JIT dispatch)
4. gemm_a4w4 HIP ASM produces ~1-3% different results from submission.py callsite
5. For shapes under 100k tokens, kernel launch + Python overhead dominate

**Why a team approach:** Phase 8 was sequential — one agent doing MLA, then GEMM, then MoE.
The three kernels are independent (different files, leaderboards, and optimization strategies).
A team can parallelize research and code writing while the lead handles sequential submissions.

## Team Architecture

**Team name:** `kernel-speedrun`

| Agent | Role | File Ownership | Tools Needed |
|-------|------|----------------|-------------|
| **Lead** (main session) | Coordinates, submits via popcorn-cli, reviews results | None (reads all) | Bash (popcorn-cli), SendMessage |
| **mla-dev** | MLA kernel research + implementation | `kernels/mixed-mla/` | Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Bash |
| **gemm-dev** | GEMM kernel research + implementation | `kernels/mxfp4-mm/` | Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Bash |

**Why 3 agents, not more:**
- Only 2 kernels have meaningful improvement headroom (MoE is at 1.07x gap — diminishing returns)
- Popcorn-cli submissions are sequential (~10 min each) — the lead is the bottleneck
- More agents = more coordination overhead + linear token cost
- 3 is the sweet spot: 2 implementers saturate the submission pipeline

**Why not a dedicated research agent:**
- Each kernel's research is highly specialized (MLA: attention algorithms; GEMM: quantized matmul)
- A generalist researcher would duplicate context that kernel-specific agents already need
- Each dev agent does targeted research within their domain

### Communication Protocol

```
mla-dev ──writes code──→ kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py
         ──messages────→ Lead: "ready for submission"

gemm-dev ──writes code─→ kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py
          ──messages───→ Lead: "ready for submission"

Lead ──submits──→ popcorn-cli (sequential, ~10 min per submit)
     ──messages─→ mla-dev/gemm-dev: "results: X µs, errors: Y"
```

**Submission queue:** Lead maintains a FIFO queue. While waiting for one submission,
the other agent can be writing code. Typical cycle:
1. mla-dev signals ready → Lead submits MLA → 10 min wait
2. During wait, gemm-dev signals ready → Lead queues GEMM
3. MLA result arrives → Lead messages mla-dev → Lead submits GEMM
4. While GEMM runs, mla-dev iterates based on results

**Total Tasks:** 8 | **Done:** 0 | **Left:** 8

## Task Details

### Task 1: MLA — FP8 Score GEMM (mla-dev)

**Why:** bf16 tl.dot uses half the MFMA throughput of fp8. For compute-bound shapes (bs≤32),
fp8 scoring gives ~1.5-2x speedup. The input already provides `kv_data["fp8"]`.

**Approach:**
1. Read `reference.py:quantize_fp8()` for Q quantization pattern
2. Inside `_mla_flash_decode` kernel: load fp8 Q + fp8 KV tiles
3. `qk = tl.dot(q1_fp8, tl.trans(kv1_fp8))` with fp32 accumulator
4. Apply `q_scale * kv_scale * SM_SCALE` after the dot product
5. Keep value accumulation in bf16 initially (Task 2 upgrades this)

**Constraint:** `tl.dot` with fp8 requires BLOCK_M ≥ 16 and BLOCK_N ≥ 16 on gfx950.
Current BLOCK_M=16, BLOCK_N=64 — both satisfy this.

### Task 2: MLA — FP8 Value Path (mla-dev)

**Why:** For bandwidth-bound shapes (bs=256, kv=8k), KV bandwidth dominates at ~288 µs (bf16).
FP8 KV halves this to ~144 µs. Using fp8 for BOTH score AND value means only ONE KV load.

**Approach:**
1. Load fp8 KV once per tile (instead of bf16 KV for values)
2. Score: `tl.dot(q_fp8, tl.trans(kv_fp8))` — already done in Task 1
3. Value: `tl.dot(p.to(fp8), kv1_fp8)` — accumulate in fp32, scale at end
4. Final output: `acc * kv_scale` → bf16

**Risk:** FP8 value accumulation may exceed rtol=1e-2 tolerance. Mitigation: if fp8 value
fails correctness, keep bf16 value path and only use fp8 for score (Task 1 alone).

### Task 3: MLA — BLOCK_N Tuning (mla-dev)

**Why:** The real VGPR bottleneck is `kv1[BLOCK_N, 512]`. At BLOCK_N=64: ~256 VGPRs (saturates budget).
At BLOCK_N=32: ~128 VGPRs (fits with accumulator). Smaller BLOCK_N trades tile efficiency for
register pressure — may eliminate spilling and improve throughput.

**Approach:**
1. Parameterize BLOCK_N as a tunable constexpr
2. Test BLOCK_N ∈ {32, 64, 128} via benchmark
3. Smaller BLOCK_N = more loop iterations but less spilling
4. Larger BLOCK_N = fewer iterations but more register pressure

### Task 4: MLA — Threshold Sweep (mla-dev)

**Why:** Current threshold=100k was set empirically in Phase 8. With fp8 and BLOCK_N changes,
the optimal threshold shifts. Need to re-benchmark.

**Approach:** After Tasks 1-3, benchmark all shapes with FlashDecoding forced on vs ref_kernel.
Set threshold at the crossover point.

### Task 5: GEMM — Fix Fused Kernel (gemm-dev)

**Why:** `submission_tritonblas_fused.py` has the Origami XCD remapping bug (non-bijective for
`total_tiles % 8 != 0`). Fixing this with group-M swizzle could unlock the fused kernel path,
eliminating ~10 µs of quant + launch overhead.

**Approach:**
1. Copy `submission_tritonblas_fused.py` → working copy
2. Replace Origami XCD block with group-M swizzle (see `tritonblas-origami-xcd-remapping-bug` skill)
3. Test correctness across all 5 GEMM shapes
4. If passes: benchmark and compare against 20.6 µs baseline

### Task 6: GEMM — CUDA Graph Wrapping (gemm-dev)

**Why:** Our ~10 µs overhead per shape = A quant launch + GEMM launch + allocation.
CUDA graphs capture the entire sequence and replay with ~2-3 µs overhead.

**Approach:**
1. Wrap `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `gemm_afp4wfp4` in a CUDA graph
2. Use `torch.cuda.CUDAGraph()` with warmup + capture + replay
3. Challenge: dynamic shapes require separate graphs per shape (5 shapes = 5 graphs)
4. Pre-warm all 5 during first invocation

**Risk:** CUDA graph capture may not work with aiter's Triton kernels (JIT compilation
happens at capture time, not replay). Test empirically.

### Task 7: GEMM — Explore CK Direct GEMM (gemm-dev)

**Why:** If neither fused kernel nor CUDA graphs work, explore Composable Kernel (CK)
for a direct fp4 GEMM path that bypasses both aiter and Triton overhead.

**Approach:**
1. Check if `ck` or `composable_kernel` is importable on MI355X runner
2. Search for fp4/mxfp4 GEMM kernels in CK Python bindings
3. If found: write a minimal submission using CK directly

### Task 8: Final Submissions (Lead)

Submit best results from both tracks to leaderboard.

## Popcorn CLI Reference

```bash
KERNELS=.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels
CLI=~/.local/bin/popcorn-cli

# MLA: test → benchmark → leaderboard
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mixed-mla $KERNELS/mixed-mla/submission.py

# GEMM: test → benchmark → leaderboard
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode test --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode benchmark --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py
$CLI submit --no-tui --mode leaderboard --gpu MI355X --leaderboard amd-mxfp4-mm $KERNELS/mxfp4-mm/submission.py
```

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|--------|------------|
| fp8 tl.dot on gfx950 not supported | Blocks Task 1-2 | Fall back to bf16 (current) |
| fp8 value exceeds tolerance | Task 2 fails | Keep bf16 value, fp8 score only |
| BLOCK_N=32 worse (more iterations) | Task 3 no improvement | Keep BLOCK_N=64 |
| Fused kernel still fails after XCD fix | Task 5 fails | Keep gemm_afp4wfp4 baseline |
| CUDA graph incompatible with aiter JIT | Task 6 fails | Skip, focus on fused kernel |
| CK not available on runner | Task 7 fails | Accept API ceiling |
| Agent coordination overhead | Slow iteration | Keep team small (3 agents) |
| Edit conflicts on same file | Corruption | Strict file ownership per agent |

For each kernel submission:

```
     1→"""
     2→MLA decode: hybrid a16w8/a8w8 dtype routing with metadata caching.
     3→
     4→Key insight: a16w8 (bf16 Q + fp8 KV) eliminates Q quantization (~25 us)
     5→but doubles Q bandwidth. Optimal strategy:
     6→  - total_kv <= 262144: a16w8 (compute/launch overhead dominant)
     7→  - total_kv > 262144:  a8w8  (bandwidth dominant, fp8 Q halves load)
     8→
     9→Metadata cached per (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, dtype_mode).
    10→Fresh output buffer each call to avoid stale data on seed changes.
    11→"""
    12→import torch
    13→from task import input_t, output_t
    14→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    15→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    16→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    17→
    18→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)
    19→V_HEAD_DIM = 512
    20→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    21→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    22→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    23→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    24→BF16_DTYPE = torch.bfloat16
    25→
    26→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    27→A16W8_THRESHOLD = 262144
    28→
    29→_cache: dict = {}
    30→
    31→
    32→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    33→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    34→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    35→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    36→    return (
    37→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    38→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    39→    )
    40→
    41→
    42→def _build_cache(bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype, qo_indptr, kv_indptr):
    43→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    44→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    45→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
    46→
    47→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    48→        bs, qseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
    49→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=True,
    50→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS, intra_batch_mode=True,
    51→    )
    52→    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [
    53→        torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info
    54→    ]
    55→
    56→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    57→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
    58→        nheads // NUM_KV_HEADS, NUM_KV_HEADS, True,
    59→        wm, wis, wi, ri, rfm, rpm,
    60→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
    61→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
    62→        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen, uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
    63→        fast_mode=True,
    64→        max_split_per_batch=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
    65→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    66→        dtype_q=q_dtype, dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
    67→    )
    68→
    69→    return {
    70→        "kv_indices": kv_indices,
    71→        "kv_last_page_len": kv_last_page_len,
    72→        "work_meta_data": wm,
    73→        "work_indptr": wi,
    74→        "work_info_set": wis,
    75→        "reduce_indptr": ri,
    76→        "reduce_final_map": rfm,
    77→        "reduce_partial_map": rpm,
    78→    }
    79→
    80→
    81→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    82→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    83→    bs = config["batch_size"]
    84→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
    85→    qseqlen = config["q_seq_len"]
    86→    nheads = config["num_heads"]
    87→
    88→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    89→    use_a16w8 = total_kv <= A16W8_THRESHOLD
    90→
    91→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
    92→    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])
    93→
    94→    if use_a16w8:
    95→        q_input = q
    96→        q_scale = None
    97→        q_dtype = BF16_DTYPE
    98→    else:
    99→        q_input, q_scale = _quantize_fp8(q)
   100→        q_dtype = FP8_DTYPE
   101→
   102→    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, use_a16w8)
   103→    if key not in _cache:
   104→        _cache[key] = _build_cache(
   105→            bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads,
   106→            q_dtype, FP8_DTYPE,
   107→            qo_indptr, kv_indptr,
   108→        )
   109→    c = _cache[key]
   110→
   111→    o = torch.empty(
   112→        (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),
   113→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda",
   114→    )
   115→
   116→    mla_decode_fwd(
   117→        q_input.view(-1, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM), kv_4d, o,
   118→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr,
   119→        c["kv_indices"], c["kv_last_page_len"],
   120→        qseqlen,
   121→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE, nhead_kv=NUM_KV_HEADS,
   122→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE, logit_cap=0.0,
   123→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   124→        q_scale=q_scale, kv_scale=kv_scale,
   125→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   126→        work_meta_data=c["work_meta_data"],
   127→        work_indptr=c["work_indptr"],
   128→        work_info_set=c["work_info_set"],
   129→        reduce_indptr=c["reduce_indptr"],
   130→        reduce_final_map=c["reduce_final_map"],
   131→        reduce_partial_map=c["reduce_partial_map"],
   132→    )
   133→    return o
   134→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→Reference implementation for MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel.
     3→
     4→Uses aiter MLA kernels (mla_decode_fwd) as the reference.
     5→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA: absorbed q (576), compressed kv_buffer (576),
     6→output v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512.
     7→
     8→The input provides:
     9→  q:       (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query (num_heads = 128 // tp)
    10→  kv_data: dict with KV cache in three formats:
    11→    "bf16":  Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)  bfloat16          — highest precision
    12→    "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale — per-tensor quantized
    13→    "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0  — block-32 quantized
    14→  The reference quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly inside ref_kernel.
    15→
    16→The reference kernel quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly and uses fp8 KV (a8w8 kernel),
    17→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16 on MI355X with negligible accuracy loss.
    18→
    19→Decode only — persistent mode with get_mla_metadata_v1.
    20→"""
    21→
    22→import torch
    23→import torch.nn.functional as F
    24→from task import input_t, output_t
    25→from utils import make_match_reference
    26→
    27→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    28→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    29→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    30→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import (
    31→    dynamic_mxfp4_quant,
    32→    mxfp4_to_f32,
    33→    e8m0_to_f32,
    34→)
    35→
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# DeepSeek R1 latent MQA constants (forward_absorb path)
    38→# https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1-0528/blob/main/config.json
    39→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    40→TOTAL_NUM_HEADS = 128
    41→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    42→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    43→QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM = 64
    44→QK_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK + QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM   # 576
    45→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK                        # 512
    46→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)
    47→
    48→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    49→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    50→
    51→# FP8 dtype (platform-specific via aiter)
    52→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    53→
    54→# Query dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    55→Q_DTYPE = "fp8"
    56→
    57→# KV cache dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    58→KV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    59→
    60→
    61→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62→# FP8 quantization (sglang style: dynamic per-tensor)
    63→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    64→
    65→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    66→    """
    67→    Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization (following sglang scaled_fp8_quant).
    68→
    69→    Args:
    70→        tensor: bf16 tensor to quantize
    71→
    72→    Returns:
    73→        (fp8_tensor, scale) where scale is a scalar float32 tensor.
    74→        Dequantize: fp8_tensor.to(bf16) * scale
    75→    """
    76→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    77→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    78→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    79→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    80→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    81→
    82→
    83→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    84→# MXFP4 quantization (aiter native: block-32, fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 dtypes)
    85→# Uses aiter.utility.fp4_utils.dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→
    88→def quantize_mxfp4(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    89→    """
    90→    MXFP4 block-wise quantization using aiter's dynamic_mxfp4_quant.
    91→
    92→    Block size = 32. Each block gets an E8M0 scale factor.
    93→    Two FP4 E2M1 values are packed per byte.
    94→
    95→    Args:
    96→        tensor: bf16 tensor of shape [B, M, N] (N must be divisible by 32)
    97→
    98→    Returns:
    99→        (fp4_data, scale_e8m0)
   100→        - fp4_data:   shape [B, M, N//2] in aiter_dtypes.fp4x2
   101→        - scale_e8m0: shape [B*M, ceil(N/32)] padded, in aiter_dtypes.fp8_e8m0
   102→    """
   103→    orig_shape = tensor.shape  # (B, M, N)
   104→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   105→
   106→    # dynamic_mxfp4_quant expects 2D: (B*M, N)
   107→    tensor_2d = tensor.reshape(B * M, N)
   108→    fp4_data_2d, scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(tensor_2d)
   109→
   110→    # Reshape fp4_data back to 3D: (B, M, N//2)
   111→    fp4_data = fp4_data_2d.view(B, M, N // 2)
   112→
   113→    return fp4_data, scale_e8m0
   114→
   115→
   116→def dequantize_mxfp4(
   117→    fp4_data: torch.Tensor,
   118→    scale_e8m0: torch.Tensor,
   119→    orig_shape: tuple,
   120→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
   121→) -> torch.Tensor:
   122→    """
   123→    Dequantize MXFP4 tensor using aiter utilities.
   124→
   125→    Note: dynamic_mxfp4_quant may pad both row and block dimensions in scale_e8m0.
   126→    We trim scales to match the actual data dimensions.
   127→
   128→    Args:
   129→        fp4_data:   packed FP4 data, shape [B, M, N//2] in fp4x2 or uint8
   130→        scale_e8m0: E8M0 block scale factors (possibly padded) in fp8_e8m0
   131→        orig_shape: original (B, M, N) for reshaping
   132→        dtype:      output dtype
   133→
   134→    Returns:
   135→        Dequantized tensor of shape orig_shape.
   136→    """
   137→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   138→    num_rows = B * M
   139→    block_size = 32
   140→    num_blocks = N // block_size  # actual blocks needed (e.g. 576/32 = 18)
   141→
   142→    # Unpack FP4 to float32: mxfp4_to_f32 expects (..., N//2) -> (..., N)
   143→    fp4_data_2d = fp4_data.reshape(num_rows, N // 2)
   144→    float_vals = mxfp4_to_f32(fp4_data_2d)  # (num_rows, N)
   145→
   146→    # Convert E8M0 scales to float32 and trim padded dimensions
   147→    scale_f32 = e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)  # (padded_rows, padded_blocks)
   148→    scale_f32 = scale_f32[:num_rows, :num_blocks]  # (num_rows, num_blocks)
   149→
   150→    # Apply block scales
   151→    float_vals_blocked = float_vals.view(num_rows, num_blocks, block_size)
   152→    scaled = float_vals_blocked * scale_f32.unsqueeze(-1)
   153→
   154→    return scaled.view(B, M, N).to(dtype)
   155→
   156→
   157→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   158→# Persistent mode metadata helpers
   159→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   160→
   161→def _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   162→    batch_size: int,
   163→    max_q_len: int,
   164→    nhead: int,
   165→    nhead_kv: int,
   166→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
   167→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
   168→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   169→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   170→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
   171→    num_kv_splits: int = NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   172→):
   173→    """Allocate and populate work buffers for persistent mla_decode_fwd."""
   174→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
   175→        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
   176→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,
   177→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits, intra_batch_mode=True,
   178→    )
   179→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
   180→    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,
   181→     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work
   182→
   183→    # Populate the metadata buffers
   184→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
   185→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   186→        nhead // nhead_kv,   # num_heads_per_head_k
   187→        nhead_kv,            # num_heads_k
   188→        True,                # is_causal
   189→        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,
   190→        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,
   191→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   192→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   193→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   194→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   195→        fast_mode=False,
   196→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   197→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   198→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   199→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   200→    )
   201→
   202→    return {
   203→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
   204→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
   205→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
   206→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
   207→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
   208→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
   209→    }
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Aiter reference kernel (decode only)
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def _aiter_mla_decode(
   217→    q: torch.Tensor,
   218→    kv_buffer: torch.Tensor,
   219→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   220→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   221→    config: dict,
   222→    q_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   223→    kv_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   224→) -> torch.Tensor:
   225→    """
   226→    MLA decode attention using aiter persistent-mode kernel.
   227→
   228→    Supports multiple Q/KV dtype combinations:
   229→      - Q_DTYPE="fp8":  fp8 Q + fp8 KV (a8w8) — fastest on MI355X
   230→      - Q_DTYPE="bf16": bf16 Q + bf16 KV (a16w16) — highest precision
   231→
   232→    q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576)  fp8 or bf16
   233→    kv_buffer:  (total_kv, 1, 576)         fp8 or bf16
   234→    q_scale:    scalar float32 (required for fp8 Q, None for bf16)
   235→    kv_scale:   scalar float32 (required for fp8 KV, None for bf16)
   236→    """
   237→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
   238→    nq = config["num_heads"]
   239→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
   240→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
   241→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
   242→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
   243→
   244→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
   245→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
   246→
   247→    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D for aiter: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)
   248→    kv_buffer_4d = kv_buffer.view(kv_buffer.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer.shape[-1])
   249→
   250→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   251→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   252→
   253→    # Build persistent-mode metadata
   254→    meta = _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   255→        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,
   256→        q.dtype, kv_buffer.dtype,
   257→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   258→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   259→    )
   260→
   261→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   262→    mla_decode_fwd(
   263→        q.view(-1, nq, dq),
   264→        kv_buffer_4d,
   265→        o,
   266→        qo_indptr,
   267→        kv_indptr,
   268→        kv_indices,
   269→        kv_last_page_len,
   270→        max_q_len,
   271→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   272→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   273→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   274→        logit_cap=0.0,
   275→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   276→        q_scale=q_scale,
   277→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   278→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   279→        **meta,
   280→    )
   281→    return o
   282→
   283→
   284→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   285→# generate_input / ref_kernel / check_implementation
   286→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   287→
   288→def generate_input(batchsize: int, qseqlen: int, kvseqlen: int, tp: int, seed: int) -> input_t:
   289→    """
   290→    Generate absorbed q and compressed kv_buffer for MLA decode.
   291→
   292→    Args:
   293→        tp: tensor parallelism degree (4 or 8). num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp.
   294→
   295→    Returns all three KV cache formats in kv_data dict:
   296→      kv_data = {
   297→        "bf16":  Tensor               — (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
   298→        "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale
   299→        "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 scale
   300→      }
   301→    """
   302→    assert TOTAL_NUM_HEADS % tp == 0, f"TOTAL_NUM_HEADS ({TOTAL_NUM_HEADS}) must be divisible by tp ({tp})"
   303→    num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp
   304→
   305→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
   306→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
   307→
   308→    total_q = batchsize * qseqlen
   309→    total_kv = batchsize * kvseqlen
   310→
   311→    # Absorbed query: (total_q, num_heads, 576) bf16
   312→    q = torch.randn(
   313→        (total_q, num_heads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   314→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   315→    ) * 0.02
   316→
   317→    # Compressed KV buffer: (total_kv, 1, 576) bf16 — the source of truth
   318→    kv_buffer_bf16 = torch.randn(
   319→        (total_kv, NUM_KV_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   320→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   321→    ) * 0.02
   322→
   323→    # Quantize KV to fp8
   324→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = quantize_fp8(kv_buffer_bf16)
   325→
   326→    # Quantize KV to mxfp4
   327→    kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = quantize_mxfp4(kv_buffer_bf16)
   328→
   329→    # All three KV formats: bf16 is a Tensor, fp8/mxfp4 are (Tensor, Tensor) tuples
   330→    kv_data = {
   331→        "bf16": kv_buffer_bf16,
   332→        "fp8": (kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8),
   333→        "mxfp4": (kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4),
   334→    }
   335→
   336→    qo_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * qseqlen
   337→    kv_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * kvseqlen
   338→
   339→    config = {
   340→        "batch_size": batchsize,
   341→        "num_heads": num_heads,
   342→        "num_kv_heads": NUM_KV_HEADS,
   343→        "qk_head_dim": QK_HEAD_DIM,
   344→        "kv_lora_rank": KV_LORA_RANK,
   345→        "qk_rope_head_dim": QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM,
   346→        "v_head_dim": V_HEAD_DIM,
   347→        "q_seq_len": qseqlen,
   348→        "kv_seq_len": kvseqlen,
   349→        "sm_scale": SM_SCALE,
   350→    }
   351→
   352→    return (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
   353→
   354→
   355→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   356→    """Reference MLA decode attention. Uses Q_DTYPE and KV_DTYPE to select kernel variant."""
   357→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   358→
   359→    # Resolve Q
   360→    if Q_DTYPE == "fp8":
   361→        q_input, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
   362→    else:
   363→        q_input, q_scale = q, None
   364→
   365→    # Resolve KV
   366→    if KV_DTYPE == "fp8":
   367→        kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   368→        kv_input = kv_buffer_fp8
   369→    else:
   370→        kv_input, kv_scale = kv_data["bf16"], None
   371→
   372→    return _aiter_mla_decode(
   373→        q_input, kv_input, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config,
   374→        q_scale=q_scale, kv_scale=kv_scale,
   375→    )
   376→
   377→
   378→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
   379→
```

```
     1→import torch
     2→from typing import TypeVar, TypedDict, Union
     3→
     4→# DeepSeek R1 MLA forward_absorb format:
     5→#
     6→# Input: (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
     7→#   q:          (total_q, num_heads, qk_head_dim)   bfloat16
     8→#               num_heads = 128 // tp (tp=4 → 32, tp=8 → 16)
     9→#   kv_data:    dict with three KV cache formats:
    10→#     "bf16":   Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)           bfloat16
    11→#     "fp8":    (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 (total_kv, 1, 576) + scalar scale
    12→#     "mxfp4":  (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 (total_kv, 1, 288) + fp8_e8m0 scale
    13→#   qo_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)                    int32
    14→#   kv_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)                    int32
    15→#   config:     dict with MLA parameters (includes num_heads computed from tp)
    16→#
    17→# where qk_head_dim = kv_lora_rank + qk_rope_head_dim = 512 + 64 = 576
    18→#
    19→# Output: attention output tensor (total_q, num_heads, v_head_dim) bfloat16
    20→#   where v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512
    21→#
    22→# The kv_buffer stores the compressed KV representation:
    23→#   - Full 576 dims used as keys (for Q@K^T score computation)
    24→#   - First 512 dims (kv_lora_rank) used as values (for output computation)
    25→
    26→input_t = TypeVar(
    27→    "input_t",
    28→    bound=tuple[torch.Tensor, dict, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, dict],
    29→)
    30→output_t = TypeVar("output_t", bound=torch.Tensor)
    31→
    32→
    33→class TestSpec(TypedDict):
    34→    batchsize: int
    35→    qseqlen: int
    36→    kvseqlen: int
    37→    tp: int
    38→    seed: int
    39→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→MXFP4 GEMM: dynamic_mxfp4_quant + e8m0_shuffle + gemm_a4w4 (ASM).
     3→
     4→Uses the patched Triton quant path (PR #975) which bypasses the buggy
     5→get_triton_quant dispatch. HIP ASM kernel for maximum throughput.
     6→"""
     7→import torch
     8→from task import input_t, output_t
     9→from aiter import dtypes
    10→import aiter
    11→from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    12→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle
    13→
    14→
    15→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    16→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    17→    x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())
    18→    A_q = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
    19→    A_scale_sh = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
    20→    return aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    21→        A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
    22→        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,
    23→    )
    24→
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The MLA submission uses aiter's `mla_decode_fwd` with a hybrid a16w8/a8w8 strategy, routing based on `total_kv <= 262144`. The ~130µs overhead comes from Python dispatch + metadata computation + JIT compilation inside aiter.
- The GEMM submission is a clean 23-line pipeline: `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` → `e8m0_shuffle` → `gemm_a4w4`. The ~20µs overhead is split between two kernel launches plus Python dispatch.
- The plan's core insight is that bypassing Python entirely via `hiprtc` (HIP Runtime Compilation) could eliminate the ~130µs dispatch overhead, getting closer to the 4.3µs leader. This is the same technique used by the competition leaders.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **PRIME skills** in Cohezion are markdown specifications that define how an AI agent should approach a specific domain. They follow a structured format: Objective → Input → Output → Constraints → Refinement Criteria. The compound engineering loop reads these to drive skill execution and refinement.
- **hiprtc** (HIP Runtime Compilation) is AMD's equivalent of NVIDIA's nvrtc — it compiles HIP C++ kernel source strings at runtime into GPU modules. This eliminates Python-level dispatch overhead by keeping the entire kernel lifecycle in native code, which is how the competition leaders achieve <5µs latencies.
- The **local model delegation** pattern is architecturally interesting: Claude acts as an architect writing specs (~500 tokens), while a LoRA-finetuned local model generates bulk kernel code (~2000 tokens). This is a 10x token efficiency gain for iterative kernel development.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me create all three files now.

```json
  "content": "# HIP Kernel Engineer\n\n## Objective\n\nGenerate HIP C++ GPU kernels for AMD MI355X (gfx950, CDNA4) that compile\nvia hiprtc and launch via hipModuleLaunchKernel from Python. Designed for\ndelegation to local finetuned models (qwen3-coder:30b + LoRA) with Claude\nacting as architect and reviewer.\n\n## Architecture: Claude as Orchestrator\n\n```\nClaude Code (architect)\n  ├─ Designs kernel specs (HIP C++ structure, MFMA patterns, memory layout)\n  ├─ Writes structured prompts for local model\n  ├─ Reviews local model output (correctness check)\n  ├─ Submits via popcorn-cli\n  │\nLocal Finetuned Model (kernel engineer) — runs on Strix Halo (128GB RAM)\n  ├─ Drafts HIP C++ kernel source from spec\n  ├─ Iterates on correctness (compile errors, numeric issues)\n  ├─ Generates submission.py variants\n  └─ Runs via Ollama API or cohezion compound system\n```\n\n## Input\n\n- **Kernel specification**: workload description, data layout, grid/block config\n- **Target architecture**: MI355X gfx950, 256 CUs x 8 XCDs, 288GB HBM3E @ 8 TB/s\n- **MFMA specs**: 256 VGPRs + 256 AGPRs per SIMD, 64KB LDS per CU\n- **Correctness requirements**: reference output tensors, rtol/atol tolerances\n\n## Output\n\n- Complete HIP C++ kernel source as a Python string constant\n- `submission.py` wrapper with compile/load/launch logic via hiprtc or ctypes\n- Expected performance analysis (arithmetic intensity, memory bandwidth utilization)\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Must compile with `hiprtcCompileProgram --gpu-architecture=gfx950`\n- All code in a single `submission.py` file (popcorn-cli requirement)\n- Fallback to `ref_kernel` on any compilation or launch error\n- No external files — kernel source embedded as Python string\n- Must pass correctness: `rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2` against reference\n\n## MI355X Architecture Reference\n\n| Property | Value |\n|----------|-------|\n| CUs | 256 (8 XCDs x 32 CUs) |\n| Peak FP4 | 9.2 PFLOPS |\n| Peak FP8 | 4.6 PFLOPS |\n| Peak BF16 | 2.3 PFLOPS |\n| HBM3E | 288 GB @ 8 TB/s |\n| VGPRs/SIMD | 256 + 256 AGPRs |\n| LDS/CU | 64 KB |\n| Key MFMA (bf16) | `__builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_16x16x16_bf16` |\n| Key MFMA (fp4) | `__builtin_amdgcn_mfma_f32_32x32x64_fp4` (gfx950 only) |\n\n## Kernel Writing Prompt Template\n\nWhen delegating to the local model, use this structured prompt:\n\n```\nTASK: Write a HIP C++ kernel for [WORKLOAD].\n\nTARGET: AMD MI355X (gfx950, CDNA4)\nGRID: ({grid_x}, {grid_y}, {grid_z})\nBLOCK: ({block_x}, {block_y}, {block_z})\n\nDATA LAYOUT:\n- Input A: [{shape}] {dtype} at pointer `a`\n- Input B: [{shape}] {dtype} at pointer `b`\n- Output C: [{shape}] {dtype} at pointer `c`\n- Scalars: {list of scalar params}\n\nALGORITHM:\n1. [Step-by-step algorithm description]\n2. [Memory access pattern]\n3. [Reduction strategy if applicable]\n\nCONSTRAINTS:\n- Use __shared__ memory for [specific tiles]\n- Use MFMA intrinsic: [specific intrinsic]\n- Coalesce global memory reads along [dimension]\n- Target occupancy: [waves/CU]\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT:\n- Complete __global__ void kernel function\n- Include all necessary headers (#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>)\n- Use extern \"C\" linkage for hiprtc compatibility\n```\n\n## hiprtc Compilation Pattern\n\n```python\nimport ctypes\n\nKERNEL_SOURCE = r'''\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\nextern \"C\" __global__ void my_kernel(float* out, const float* in, int N) {\n    int idx = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (idx < N) out[idx] = in[idx];\n}\n'''\n\ndef compile_and_load(source: str, kernel_name: str, arch: str = \"gfx950\"):\n    \"\"\"Compile HIP C++ source via hiprtc and return kernel function.\"\"\"\n    hiprtc = ctypes.CDLL(\"libhiprtc.so\")\n    hip = ctypes.CDLL(\"libamdhip64.so\")\n\n    # Create program\n    prog = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    hiprtc.hiprtcCreateProgram(\n        ctypes.byref(prog),\n        source.encode(), b\"kernel.hip\",\n        0, None, None,\n    )\n\n    # Compile\n    opts = [f\"--gpu-architecture={arch}\".encode()]\n    opts_arr = (ctypes.c_char_p * len(opts))(*opts)\n    result = hiprtc.hiprtcCompileProgram(prog, len(opts), opts_arr)\n    if result != 0:\n        # Get compile log\n        log_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n        hiprtc.hiprtcGetProgramLogSize(prog, ctypes.byref(log_size))\n        log = ctypes.create_string_buffer(log_size.value)\n        hiprtc.hiprtcGetProgramLog(prog, log)\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hiprtc compile failed: {log.value.decode()}\")\n\n    # Get code\n    code_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n    hiprtc.hiprtcGetCodeSize(prog, ctypes.byref(code_size))\n    code = ctypes.create_string_buffer(code_size.value)\n    hiprtc.hiprtcGetCode(prog, code)\n\n    # Load module + get function\n    module = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    hip.hipModuleLoadData(ctypes.byref(module), code)\n    func = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    hip.hipModuleGetFunction(\n        ctypes.byref(func), module, kernel_name.encode(),\n    )\n\n    return module, func\n```\n\n## Submission.py Wrapper Pattern\n\n```python\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    # Unpack input\n    ...\n\n    # Compile kernel (cached after first call)\n    global _module, _func\n    if _func is None:\n        _module, _func = compile_and_load(KERNEL_SOURCE, \"kernel_name\")\n\n    # Launch kernel\n    hip = ctypes.CDLL(\"libamdhip64.so\")\n    args = [\n        ctypes.c_void_p(output.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(input.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_int(N),\n    ]\n    args_arr = (ctypes.c_void_p * len(args))(\n        *[ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(a), ctypes.c_void_p) for a in args]\n    )\n\n    hip.hipModuleLaunchKernel(\n        _func,\n        grid_x, grid_y, grid_z,    # grid dimensions\n        block_x, block_y, block_z,  # block dimensions\n        shared_mem_bytes, None,      # shared mem, stream\n        args_arr, None,              # kernel args\n    )\n\n    return output\n```\n\n## Refinement Criteria\n\n| Metric | Target | Method |\n|--------|--------|--------|\n| Correctness | rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2 | popcorn-cli --mode test |\n| MLA latency | < 15 µs geomean | popcorn-cli --mode benchmark |\n| GEMM latency | < 15 µs geomean | popcorn-cli --mode benchmark |\n| Compile time | < 5 s first call | Cache compiled module |\n| Code size | < 500 LOC kernel | Single submission.py |\n\n## Iteration Protocol\n\n1. Claude writes kernel spec (this document's template)\n2. Local model generates HIP C++ kernel source\n3. Claude reviews for: correct indexing, memory coalescing, MFMA usage\n4. Submit via popcorn-cli --mode test (correctness gate)\n5. If fails: Claude diagnoses error, writes refined spec, goto 2\n6. If passes: Submit --mode benchmark, analyze results\n7. If improved: Submit --mode leaderboard\n8. Record learnings, refine spec for next iteration\n\n## Related Skills\n\n- `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla` — VGPR budget analysis\n- `deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap` — MLA architectural analysis\n- `popcorn-cli-amd-kernel-submission` — Submission workflow\n- `triton-fp4-inline-quantization` — FP4 quant patterns\n- `CUDA_GRAPH_DISPATCH_PRIME` — Graph-based dispatch patterns\n- `TRITON_BIT_ACCURATE_QUANT_PRIME` — Quantization accuracy patterns\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nLoRA finetune script for building a local HIP kernel-writing model.\n\nBase model: qwen3-coder:30b (best code model fitting 128GB unified RAM)\nMethod: QLoRA finetune on collected HIP/CUDA kernel corpus\nFocus: HIP C++ kernel syntax, MFMA intrinsics, hiprtc patterns, MLA decode\n\nUsage (run locally on Strix Halo, zero Claude tokens):\n    python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py collect   # Gather training data\n    python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py train      # Run LoRA finetune\n    python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py export     # Export to GGUF\n    python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py deploy     # Register with Ollama\n    python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py all        # Full pipeline\n\nRequirements:\n    pip install unsloth transformers datasets peft accelerate\n    pip install llama-cpp-python  # for GGUF conversion\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport re\nimport shutil\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nPROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nTRAINING_DATA_DIR = Path(\"/tmp/kernel-training-data\")\nLORA_OUTPUT_DIR = Path(\"/tmp/kernel-model-lora\")\nGGUF_OUTPUT_DIR = Path(\"/tmp/kernel-model-gguf\")\n\nKERNEL_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / \"research\" / \"challenges\" / \"luma_amd_speedrun\" / \"kernels\"\n\nBASE_MODEL = \"Qwen/Qwen3-Coder-30B\"\nOLLAMA_MODEL_NAME = \"hip-kernel-dev\"\n\nTRAINING_REPOS = [\n    (\"https://github.com/HazyResearch/ThunderKittens.git\", \"thunderkittens-data\"),\n    (\"https://github.com/ROCm/rocm-examples.git\", \"rocm-examples\"),\n]\n\nHIP_EXTENSIONS = {\".hip\", \".cu\", \".cpp\", \".cxx\", \".h\", \".hpp\"}\nPYTHON_EXTENSIONS = {\".py\"}\n\n\ndef collect_data() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Phase 0A: Collect HIP/CUDA kernel training data from multiple sources.\"\"\"\n    TRAINING_DATA_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    samples: list[dict[str, str]] = []\n\n    # 1. Clone external repos\n    for repo_url, dirname in TRAINING_REPOS:\n        dest = TRAINING_DATA_DIR / dirname\n        if not dest.exists():\n            print(f\"Cloning {repo_url} -> {dest}\")\n            subprocess.run(\n                [\"git\", \"clone\", \"--depth\", \"1\", repo_url, str(dest)],\n                check=True,\n            )\n        else:\n            print(f\"Already cloned: {dest}\")\n\n        # Extract HIP/CUDA source files\n        for ext in HIP_EXTENSIONS:\n            for f in dest.rglob(f\"*{ext}\"):\n                content = f.read_text(errors=\"ignore\")\n                if len(content) < 100 or len(content) > 50_000:\n                    continue\n                if any(kw in content for kw in [\"__global__\", \"__device__\", \"MFMA\", \"mfma\", \"threadIdx\"]):\n                    samples.append({\n                        \"source\": str(f.relative_to(TRAINING_DATA_DIR)),\n                        \"type\": \"hip_kernel\",\n                        \"content\": content,\n                    })\n\n    # 2. Collect aiter source (if installed)\n    aiter_path = _find_aiter_source()\n    if aiter_path:\n        print(f\"Collecting aiter source from {aiter_path}\")\n        for ext in HIP_EXTENSIONS | PYTHON_EXTENSIONS:\n            for f in aiter_path.rglob(f\"*{ext}\"):\n                content = f.read_text(errors=\"ignore\")\n                if len(content) < 100 or len(content) > 50_000:\n                    continue\n                if any(kw in content for kw in [\n                    \"mla_decode\", \"gemm_a4w4\", \"mfma\", \"__global__\",\n                    \"triton.jit\", \"dynamic_mxfp4\", \"flash_attn\",\n                ]):\n                    samples.append({\n                        \"source\": f\"aiter/{f.relative_to(aiter_path)}\",\n                        \"type\": \"aiter_kernel\",\n                        \"content\": content,\n                    })\n\n    # 3. Our own submission variants (11 phases of kernel code)\n    if KERNEL_DIR.exists():\n        print(f\"Collecting our kernels from {KERNEL_DIR}\")\n        for f in KERNEL_DIR.rglob(\"*.py\"):\n            content = f.read_text(errors=\"ignore\")\n            if \"custom_kernel\" in content or \"ref_kernel\" in content:\n                samples.append({\n                    \"source\": f\"ours/{f.relative_to(KERNEL_DIR)}\",\n                    \"type\": \"submission\",\n                    \"content\": content,\n                })\n\n    # 4. Format as instruction-completion pairs for finetuning\n    formatted = _format_training_samples(samples)\n\n    output_file = TRAINING_DATA_DIR / \"training_data.jsonl\"\n    with open(output_file, \"w\") as fh:\n        for sample in formatted:\n            fh.write(json.dumps(sample) + \"\\n\")\n\n    print(f\"Collected {len(samples)} raw samples -> {len(formatted)} training pairs\")\n    print(f\"Written to {output_file}\")\n\n\ndef _find_aiter_source() -> Path | None:\n    \"\"\"Locate aiter package source directory.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [\"pip\", \"show\", \"aiter\"],\n            capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,\n        )\n        for line in result.stdout.splitlines():\n            if line.startswith(\"Location:\"):\n                loc = Path(line.split(\": \", 1)[1].strip()) / \"aiter\"\n                if loc.exists():\n                    return loc\n    except (subprocess.CalledProcessError, FileNotFoundError):\n        pass\n\n    # Fallback: check common ROCm paths\n    for candidate in [Path(\"/opt/rocm/lib/python3/aiter\"), Path(\"/usr/lib/python3/aiter\")]:\n        if candidate.exists():\n            return candidate\n    return None\n\n\ndef _format_training_samples(samples: list[dict[str, str]]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Convert raw source files into instruction-completion pairs.\"\"\"\n    formatted = []\n\n    for sample in samples:\n        content = sample[\"content\"]\n\n        if sample[\"type\"] == \"hip_kernel\":\n            # Extract kernel functions and create spec->implementation pairs\n            kernels = _extract_kernel_signatures(content)\n            for sig, body in kernels:\n                formatted.append({\n                    \"instruction\": (\n                        f\"Write a HIP C++ kernel with the following signature:\\n\"\n                        f\"```cpp\\n{sig}\\n```\\n\"\n                        f\"Target: AMD MI355X (gfx950, CDNA4). \"\n                        f\"Use appropriate MFMA intrinsics and memory coalescing.\"\n                    ),\n                    \"output\": f\"```cpp\\n{body}\\n```\",\n                })\n\n        elif sample[\"type\"] == \"submission\":\n            # Use submission.py files as completion examples\n            formatted.append({\n                \"instruction\": (\n                    \"Write a GPU kernel submission.py for the Luma AMD Speedrun \"\n                    \"competition targeting MI355X (gfx950). The submission must \"\n                    \"define `custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t` and pass \"\n                    \"correctness checks with rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2.\"\n                ),\n                \"output\": content,\n            })\n\n        elif sample[\"type\"] == \"aiter_kernel\":\n            formatted.append({\n                \"instruction\": (\n                    \"Explain and reproduce the following AMD GPU kernel code. \"\n                    \"Include comments about memory access patterns, MFMA usage, \"\n                    \"and performance characteristics on CDNA4 architecture.\"\n                ),\n                \"output\": content,\n            })\n\n    return formatted\n\n\ndef _extract_kernel_signatures(source: str) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Extract __global__ kernel signatures and bodies from HIP/CUDA source.\"\"\"\n    kernels = []\n    pattern = re.compile(\n        r'(__global__\\s+\\w+\\s+\\w+\\s*\\([^)]*\\))\\s*\\{',\n        re.MULTILINE,\n    )\n\n    for match in pattern.finditer(source):\n        sig = match.group(1)\n        start = match.end()\n        # Find matching closing brace (simple brace counting)\n        depth = 1\n        pos = start\n        while pos < len(source) and depth > 0:\n            if source[pos] == '{':\n                depth += 1\n            elif source[pos] == '}':\n                depth -= 1\n            pos += 1\n\n        body = sig + \" {\\n\" + source[start:pos]\n        kernels.append((sig, body))\n\n    return kernels\n\n\ndef train() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Phase 0B: Run QLoRA finetune on collected kernel corpus.\"\"\"\n    training_file = TRAINING_DATA_DIR / \"training_data.jsonl\"\n    if not training_file.exists():\n        print(\"Training data not found. Run 'collect' first.\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    LORA_OUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    try:\n        from unsloth import FastLanguageModel\n        from datasets import Dataset\n        from trl import SFTTrainer\n        from transformers import TrainingArguments\n    except ImportError:\n        print(\"Missing dependencies. Install with:\")\n        print(\"  pip install unsloth transformers datasets trl peft accelerate\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    # Load training data\n    samples = []\n    with open(training_file) as fh:\n        for line in fh:\n            samples.append(json.loads(line))\n\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(samples)} training samples\")\n\n    # Format as chat template\n    def format_sample(sample: dict) -> dict:\n        return {\n            \"text\": (\n                f\"### Instruction:\\n{sample['instruction']}\\n\\n\"\n                f\"### Response:\\n{sample['output']}\"\n            )\n        }\n\n    dataset = Dataset.from_list([format_sample(s) for s in samples])\n\n    # Load base model with 4-bit quantization\n    model, tokenizer = FastLanguageModel.from_pretrained(\n        model_name=BASE_MODEL,\n        max_seq_length=8192,\n        dtype=None,  # auto-detect\n        load_in_4bit=True,\n    )\n\n    # Add LoRA adapters\n    model = FastLanguageModel.get_peft_model(\n        model,\n        r=64,\n        target_modules=[\n            \"q_proj\", \"k_proj\", \"v_proj\", \"o_proj\",\n            \"gate_proj\", \"up_proj\", \"down_proj\",\n        ],\n        lora_alpha=128,\n        lora_dropout=0.05,\n        bias=\"none\",\n        use_gradient_checkpointing=\"unsloth\",\n    )\n\n    # Training arguments\n    training_args = TrainingArguments(\n        per_device_train_batch_size=1,\n        gradient_accumulation_steps=4,\n        warmup_steps=10,\n        num_train_epochs=3,\n        learning_rate=2e-4,\n        fp16=True,\n        logging_steps=10,\n        output_dir=str(LORA_OUTPUT_DIR),\n        save_strategy=\"epoch\",\n        optim=\"adamw_8bit\",\n        seed=42,\n    )\n\n    # Train\n    trainer = SFTTrainer(\n        model=model,\n        tokenizer=tokenizer,\n        train_dataset=dataset,\n        dataset_text_field=\"text\",\n        max_seq_length=8192,\n        args=training_args,\n    )\n\n    print(\"Starting LoRA finetune...\")\n    trainer.train()\n\n    # Save LoRA weights\n    model.save_pretrained(str(LORA_OUTPUT_DIR / \"final\"))\n    tokenizer.save_pretrained(str(LORA_OUTPUT_DIR / \"final\"))\n    print(f\"LoRA weights saved to {LORA_OUTPUT_DIR / 'final'}\")\n\n\ndef export_gguf() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Phase 0C: Convert LoRA model to GGUF for Ollama.\"\"\"\n    lora_path = LORA_OUTPUT_DIR / \"final\"\n    if not lora_path.exists():\n        print(\"LoRA weights not found. Run 'train' first.\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    GGUF_OUTPUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    try:\n        from unsloth import FastLanguageModel\n    except ImportError:\n        print(\"Missing unsloth. Install with: pip install unsloth\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    model, tokenizer = FastLanguageModel.from_pretrained(\n        model_name=str(lora_path),\n        max_seq_length=8192,\n        dtype=None,\n        load_in_4bit=True,\n    )\n\n    gguf_path = str(GGUF_OUTPUT_DIR / \"hip-kernel-dev-q4_k_m.gguf\")\n    model.save_pretrained_gguf(\n        gguf_path,\n        tokenizer,\n        quantization_method=\"q4_k_m\",\n    )\n    print(f\"GGUF model saved to {gguf_path}\")\n\n\ndef deploy_ollama() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Phase 0C: Register finetuned GGUF model with Ollama.\"\"\"\n    gguf_files = list(GGUF_OUTPUT_DIR.glob(\"*.gguf\"))\n    if not gguf_files:\n        print(\"No GGUF files found. Run 'export' first.\")\n        sys.exit(1)\n\n    gguf_path = gguf_files[0]\n\n    # Create Modelfile\n    modelfile_content = f\"\"\"FROM {gguf_path}\n\nPARAMETER temperature 0.2\nPARAMETER top_p 0.9\nPARAMETER num_ctx 8192\nPARAMETER stop \"### Instruction:\"\nPARAMETER stop \"### Response:\"\n\nSYSTEM \\\"\\\"\\\"You are a HIP C++ GPU kernel engineer specializing in AMD MI355X\n(gfx950, CDNA4) architecture. You write high-performance kernels using MFMA\nintrinsics, optimized memory access patterns, and hiprtc-compatible code.\nAlways use extern \"C\" linkage for hiprtc compatibility.\nTarget architecture: gfx950 with 256 CUs, 288GB HBM3E @ 8 TB/s.\\\"\\\"\\\"\n\"\"\"\n\n    modelfile_path = GGUF_OUTPUT_DIR / \"Modelfile\"\n    modelfile_path.write_text(modelfile_content)\n\n    # Check if Ollama is available\n    if not shutil.which(\"ollama\"):\n        print(\"Ollama not found in PATH. Install from https://ollama.com\")\n        print(f\"Modelfile written to {modelfile_path}\")\n        print(f\"Run manually: ollama create {OLLAMA_MODEL_NAME} -f {modelfile_path}\")\n        return\n\n    print(f\"Creating Ollama model '{OLLAMA_MODEL_NAME}'...\")\n    subprocess.run(\n        [\"ollama\", \"create\", OLLAMA_MODEL_NAME, \"-f\", str(modelfile_path)],\n        check=True,\n    )\n    print(f\"Model '{OLLAMA_MODEL_NAME}' registered with Ollama\")\n    print(f\"Test with: ollama run {OLLAMA_MODEL_NAME} 'Write a HIP vector add kernel'\")\n\n\ndef main() -> None:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Build a local HIP kernel-writing model via LoRA finetune\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"stage\",\n        choices=[\"collect\", \"train\", \"export\", \"deploy\", \"all\"],\n        help=\"Pipeline stage to run\",\n    )\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    stages = {\n        \"collect\": collect_data,\n        \"train\": train,\n        \"export\": export_gguf,\n        \"deploy\": deploy_ollama,\n    }\n\n    if args.stage == \"all\":\n        for name, func in stages.items():\n            print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n            print(f\"Stage: {name}\")\n            print(f\"{'='*60}\")\n            func()\n    else:\n        stages[args.stage]()\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
```

Now the hiprtc environment probe (Task 3).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The probe submission is critical because it answers the **decision gate**: can we use hiprtc on the MI355X runner? There are three possible paths to access hiprtc: (1) `hip-python` package (`from hip import hip, hiprtc`), (2) `ctypes.CDLL("libhiprtc.so")` for direct C library access, (3) `hipcc` compiler available as a subprocess. The probe tests all three since we don't know what the runner environment looks like.
- The probe must still conform to the submission format (`custom_kernel(data) -> output_t`) since it's submitted via popcorn-cli. It delegates to `ref_kernel` for correctness while printing probe diagnostics to stdout.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

               result[\"compile_test\"] = \"SUCCESS: noop kernel compiled for gfx950\"\n                else:\n                    # Get compile log\n                    log_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n                    hiprtc.hiprtcGetProgramLogSize(prog, ctypes.byref(log_size))\n                    log = ctypes.create_string_buffer(log_size.value)\n                    hiprtc.hiprtcGetProgramLog(prog, log)\n                    result[\"compile_test\"] = f\"COMPILE_FAIL (rc={rc}): {log.value.decode()}\"\n                hiprtc.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n            else:\n                result[\"compile_test\"] = f\"CREATE_FAIL (rc={rc})\"\n        except Exception as e:\n            result[\"compile_test\"] = f\"ERROR: {e}\"\n\n    return result\n\n\ndef _probe_hipcc() -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Test hipcc compiler availability.\"\"\"\n    result = {\"available\": False, \"version\": None, \"path\": None, \"error\": None}\n    hipcc = shutil.which(\"hipcc\")\n    if hipcc:\n        result[\"available\"] = True\n        result[\"path\"] = hipcc\n        try:\n            ver = subprocess.run(\n                [hipcc, \"--version\"],\n                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10,\n            )\n            result[\"version\"] = ver.stdout.strip()[:200]\n        except Exception as e:\n            result[\"version\"] = f\"error: {e}\"\n    else:\n        result[\"error\"] = \"hipcc not found in PATH\"\n    return result\n\n\ndef _probe_torch_arch() -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Test torch CUDA/ROCm arch support.\"\"\"\n    result = {\n        \"cuda_available\": torch.cuda.is_available(),\n        \"device_name\": None,\n        \"arch_list\": None,\n        \"rocm_version\": None,\n    }\n    if torch.cuda.is_available():\n        result[\"device_name\"] = torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)\n        try:\n            result[\"arch_list\"] = torch.cuda.get_arch_list()\n        except Exception:\n            result[\"arch_list\"] = \"unavailable\"\n    try:\n        result[\"rocm_version\"] = torch.version.hip\n    except AttributeError:\n        result[\"rocm_version\"] = \"not ROCm build\"\n    return result\n\n\ndef _probe_env() -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Check relevant environment variables.\"\"\"\n    keys = [\n        \"ROCM_HOME\", \"ROCM_PATH\", \"HIP_PATH\", \"HSA_OVERRIDE_GFX_VERSION\",\n        \"HIPRTC_COMPILE_OPTIONS_APPEND\", \"HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES\",\n        \"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\", \"GPU_DEVICE_ORDINAL\",\n    ]\n    return {k: os.environ.get(k, \"NOT SET\") for k in keys}\n\n\ndef _probe_shared_libs() -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Check which HIP-related shared libraries exist.\"\"\"\n    search_dirs = [\n        \"/opt/rocm/lib\", \"/usr/lib\", \"/usr/lib64\",\n        \"/opt/rocm/hip/lib\", \"/usr/local/lib\",\n    ]\n    target_libs = [\n        \"libhiprtc.so\", \"libamdhip64.so\", \"libhsa-runtime64.so\",\n        \"libamd_comgr.so\", \"librocblas.so\",\n    ]\n    found = {}\n    for lib in target_libs:\n        paths = []\n        for d in search_dirs:\n            candidate = os.path.join(d, lib)\n            if os.path.exists(candidate):\n                paths.append(candidate)\n        # Also check ldconfig\n        try:\n            ld = subprocess.run(\n                [\"ldconfig\", \"-p\"],\n                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5,\n            )\n            for line in ld.stdout.splitlines():\n                if lib in line and \"=>\" in line:\n                    path = line.split(\"=>\")[-1].strip()\n                    if path not in paths:\n                        paths.append(path)\n        except Exception:\n            pass\n        found[lib] = paths if paths else [\"NOT FOUND\"]\n    return found\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"Run environment probes then delegate to ref_kernel.\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"HIPRTC ENVIRONMENT PROBE — Phase A\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    probes = {\n        \"hip_python\": _probe_hip_python,\n        \"ctypes_hiprtc\": _probe_ctypes_hiprtc,\n        \"hipcc\": _probe_hipcc,\n        \"torch_arch\": _probe_torch_arch,\n        \"env_vars\": _probe_env,\n        \"shared_libs\": _probe_shared_libs,\n    }\n\n    results = {}\n    for name, probe_fn in probes.items():\n        print(f\"\\n--- {name} ---\")\n        try:\n            result = probe_fn()\n            results[name] = result\n            for k, v in result.items():\n                print(f\"  {k}: {v}\")\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  PROBE FAILED: {e}\")\n            results[name] = {\"error\": str(e)}\n\n    # Summary\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"SUMMARY\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    hip_py = results.get(\"hip_python\", {}).get(\"available\", False)\n    ctypes_ok = (\n        results.get(\"ctypes_hiprtc\", {}).get(\"hiprtc_available\", False)\n        and results.get(\"ctypes_hiprtc\", {}).get(\"amdhip64_available\", False)\n    )\n    compile_ok = \"SUCCESS\" in str(results.get(\"ctypes_hiprtc\", {}).get(\"compile_test\", \"\"))\n    hipcc_ok = results.get(\"hipcc\", {}).get(\"available\", False)\n\n    print(f\"  hip-python available:    {'YES' if hip_py else 'NO'}\")\n    print(f\"  ctypes hiprtc available: {'YES' if ctypes_ok else 'NO'}\")\n    print(f\"  gfx950 compile test:     {'PASS' if compile_ok else 'FAIL'}\")\n    print(f\"  hipcc available:         {'YES' if hipcc_ok else 'NO'}\")\n\n    if compile_ok:\n        print(\"\\n  >>> DECISION: hiprtc path AVAILABLE — proceed to Phase B\")\n    elif ctypes_ok:\n        print(\"\\n  >>> DECISION: hiprtc libs found but compile failed — investigate arch flag\")\n    elif hipcc_ok:\n        print(\"\\n  >>> DECISION: hipcc available as fallback — use subprocess compilation\")\n    else:\n        print(\"\\n  >>> DECISION: No hiprtc path — fall back to parameter tuning only\")\n\n    print(\"=\" * 70 + \"\\n\")\n\n    # Delegate to reference kernel for correctness\n    return ref_kernel(data)\n"
```json
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **Phase B validation** tests two progressively harder operations: (B1) vector copy validates the entire hiprtc pipeline end-to-end — string compilation, module loading, kernel launch, and result verification against PyTorch. (B2) bf16 dot product validates MFMA intrinsic usage and tensor passing from PyTorch's data pointers.
- The ctypes approach wraps `hipModuleLaunchKernel` which takes kernel arguments as a `void**` array. Each element is a pointer to the actual argument value. This indirection is the key API pattern that lets us pass PyTorch tensor data pointers directly to HIP kernels.
- The kernel source is embedded as a Python raw string (`r'''...'''`) to avoid escape issues with C++ backslashes and preprocessor directives. This is the same pattern used by NVIDIA's PyCUDA.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
