```json
  "content": "\"\"\"\nPhase B: Validate hiprtc compile→load→launch pipeline.\n\nB1: Vector copy kernel — validates string→compile→load→launch→verify\nB2: BF16 dot product  — validates MFMA intrinsics + torch tensor passing\n\nFalls back to ref_kernel if hiprtc is unavailable. On success, prints\ntiming data for the hiprtc dispatch overhead measurement.\n\"\"\"\nimport ctypes\nimport time\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom reference import ref_kernel\n\n# ─── hiprtc helper ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n_hiprtc_lib = None\n_hip_lib = None\n\n\ndef _load_hip_libs():\n    \"\"\"Load hiprtc and amdhip64 shared libraries via ctypes.\"\"\"\n    global _hiprtc_lib, _hip_lib\n    if _hiprtc_lib is not None:\n        return True\n\n    for rtc_name in [\"libhiprtc.so\", \"libhiprtc.so.6\", \"libhiprtc.so.5\"]:\n        try:\n            _hiprtc_lib = ctypes.CDLL(rtc_name)\n            break\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n\n    for hip_name in [\"libamdhip64.so\", \"libamdhip64.so.6\", \"libamdhip64.so.5\"]:\n        try:\n            _hip_lib = ctypes.CDLL(hip_name)\n            break\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n\n    return _hiprtc_lib is not None and _hip_lib is not None\n\n\ndef _compile_kernel(source: str, kernel_name: str, arch: str = \"gfx950\"):\n    \"\"\"Compile HIP C++ source string → (module, function) handles.\"\"\"\n    if not _load_hip_libs():\n        raise RuntimeError(\"hiprtc/amdhip64 libraries not available\")\n\n    prog = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCreateProgram(\n        ctypes.byref(prog),\n        source.encode(), b\"kernel.hip\",\n        0, None, None,\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hiprtcCreateProgram failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    opts = [f\"--gpu-architecture={arch}\".encode()]\n    opts_arr = (ctypes.c_char_p * len(opts))(*opts)\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCompileProgram(prog, len(opts), opts_arr)\n    if rc != 0:\n        log_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetProgramLogSize(prog, ctypes.byref(log_size))\n        log_buf = ctypes.create_string_buffer(log_size.value)\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetProgramLog(prog, log_buf)\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"Compile failed: {log_buf.value.decode()}\")\n\n    code_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetCodeSize(prog, ctypes.byref(code_size))\n    code = ctypes.create_string_buffer(code_size.value)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetCode(prog, code)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n\n    module = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleLoadData(ctypes.byref(module), code)\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleLoadData failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    func = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleGetFunction(\n        ctypes.byref(func), module, kernel_name.encode(),\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleGetFunction failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    return module, func\n\n\ndef _launch_kernel(func, grid, block, shared_mem, *args):\n    \"\"\"Launch a compiled HIP kernel with the given arguments.\"\"\"\n    # Build void** args array\n    arg_ptrs = []\n    for arg in args:\n        arg_ptrs.append(ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(arg), ctypes.c_void_p))\n    args_arr = (ctypes.c_void_p * len(arg_ptrs))(*arg_ptrs)\n\n    gx, gy, gz = grid\n    bx, by, bz = block\n\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleLaunchKernel(\n        func,\n        gx, gy, gz,\n        bx, by, bz,\n        shared_mem,\n        None,  # stream (default)\n        args_arr,\n        None,  # extra\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleLaunchKernel failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n\n# ─── B1: Vector Copy ────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nVECTOR_COPY_SOURCE = r'''\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\n\nextern \"C\" __global__\nvoid vector_copy(float* __restrict__ dst,\n                 const float* __restrict__ src,\n                 int N) {\n    int idx = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (idx < N) {\n        dst[idx] = src[idx];\n    }\n}\n'''\n\n_b1_func = None\n_b1_module = None\n\n\ndef _test_vector_copy():\n    \"\"\"B1: Validate basic hiprtc pipeline with vector copy.\"\"\"\n    global _b1_func, _b1_module\n\n    N = 1024 * 1024\n    src = torch.randn(N, device=\"cuda\", dtype=torch.float32)\n    dst = torch.zeros(N, device=\"cuda\", dtype=torch.float32)\n\n    # Compile\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n    _b1_module, _b1_func = _compile_kernel(VECTOR_COPY_SOURCE, \"vector_copy\")\n    compile_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000\n    print(f\"  B1 compile time: {compile_ms:.1f} ms\")\n\n    # Launch\n    block_size = 256\n    grid_size = (N + block_size - 1) // block_size\n\n    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n\n    _launch_kernel(\n        _b1_func,\n        (grid_size, 1, 1), (block_size, 1, 1), 0,\n        ctypes.c_void_p(dst.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(src.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_int(N),\n    )\n\n    _hip_lib.hipDeviceSynchronize()\n    launch_us = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1e6\n    print(f\"  B1 launch time:  {launch_us:.1f} us\")\n\n    # Verify\n    if torch.allclose(dst, src, rtol=0, atol=0):\n        print(\"  B1 PASS: vector copy exact match\")\n        return True\n    else:\n        max_diff = (dst - src).abs().max().item()\n        print(f\"  B1 FAIL: max_diff={max_diff}\")\n        return False\n\n\n# ─── B2: BF16 Dot Product ───────────────────────────────────────────────\n\nBF16_DOT_SOURCE = r'''\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\n#include <hip/hip_bf16.h>\n\nextern \"C\" __global__\nvoid bf16_dot(float* __restrict__ out,\n              const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ a,\n              const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ b,\n              int N) {\n    // Each thread computes partial dot product over a chunk\n    int tid = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    int stride = blockDim.x * gridDim.x;\n\n    float local_sum = 0.0f;\n    for (int i = tid; i < N; i += stride) {\n        float va = __bfloat162float(a[i]);\n        float vb = __bfloat162float(b[i]);\n        local_sum += va * vb;\n    }\n\n    // Warp-level reduction\n    for (int offset = 32; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {\n        local_sum += __shfl_down(local_sum, offset);\n    }\n\n    // First thread in each warp atomically adds to output\n    if ((threadIdx.x & 63) == 0) {\n        atomicAdd(out, local_sum);\n    }\n}\n'''\n\n_b2_func = None\n_b2_module = None\n\n\ndef _test_bf16_dot():\n    \"\"\"B2: Validate bf16 tensor passing and warp-level reduction.\"\"\"\n    global _b2_func, _b2_module\n\n    N = 65536\n    a = torch.randn(N, device=\"cuda\", dtype=torch.bfloat16)\n    b = torch.randn(N, device=\"cuda\", dtype=torch.bfloat16)\n    out = torch.zeros(1, device=\"cuda\", dtype=torch.float32)\n\n    # Compile\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n    _b2_module, _b2_func = _compile_kernel(BF16_DOT_SOURCE, \"bf16_dot\")\n    compile_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000\n    print(f\"  B2 compile time: {compile_ms:.1f} ms\")\n\n    # Launch\n    block_size = 256\n    grid_size = min(256, (N + block_size - 1) // block_size)\n\n    torch.cuda.synchronize()\n    t0 = time.perf_counter()\n\n    _launch_kernel(\n        _b2_func,\n        (grid_size, 1, 1), (block_size, 1, 1), 0,\n        ctypes.c_void_p(out.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(a.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(b.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_int(N),\n    )\n\n    _hip_lib.hipDeviceSynchronize()\n    launch_us = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1e6\n    print(f\"  B2 launch time:  {launch_us:.1f} us\")\n\n    # Compare against PyTorch reference\n    ref = (a.float() @ b.float()).item()\n    got = out.item()\n    rel_err = abs(got - ref) / (abs(ref) + 1e-8)\n\n    if rel_err < 0.02:  # 2% tolerance for bf16 accumulation\n        print(f\"  B2 PASS: dot={got:.4f}, ref={ref:.4f}, rel_err={rel_err:.6f}\")\n        return True\n    else:\n        print(f\"  B2 FAIL: dot={got:.4f}, ref={ref:.4f}, rel_err={rel_err:.6f}\")\n        return False\n\n\n# ─── Submission entry point ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n_validated = None\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"Run hiprtc validation, then delegate to ref_kernel.\"\"\"\n    global _validated\n\n    if _validated is None:\n        print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n        print(\"HIPRTC VALIDATION — Phase B\")\n        print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n        b1_ok = False\n        b2_ok = False\n\n        try:\n            if not _load_hip_libs():\n                print(\"  hiprtc/amdhip64 not available — skipping validation\")\n                _validated = False\n            else:\n                print(\"\\n  B1: Vector Copy\")\n                b1_ok = _test_vector_copy()\n\n                print(\"\\n  B2: BF16 Dot Product\")\n                b2_ok = _test_bf16_dot()\n\n                _validated = b1_ok and b2_ok\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"  VALIDATION ERROR: {e}\")\n            _validated = False\n\n        print(f\"\\n  Overall: {'PASS' if _validated else 'FAIL'}\")\n        if _validated:\n            print(\"  >>> hiprtc pipeline validated — proceed to Phase C\")\n        else:\n            print(\"  >>> hiprtc pipeline NOT available — using ref_kernel fallback\")\n        print(\"=\" * 70 + \"\\n\")\n\n    return ref_kernel(data)\n"
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The MLA decode kernel is the hardest optimization target (16x gap to leader). The workload is: `Q[total_q, nheads, 576] @ KV[total_kv, 1, 576]^T → softmax → @V[:,:512]`. It's MQA (multi-query attention) with 16 Q heads sharing 1 KV head, and `qseqlen=1` (decode mode).
- **Online softmax** is critical here — the standard two-pass approach (compute max, then exp/sum, then normalize) requires storing all attention scores. Online softmax computes the running max and denominator in a single pass, reducing memory writes by ~50%. This is the technique Flash Attention uses.
- The HIP C++ kernel starts with a **naive bf16 implementation** (C1 in the plan) to validate correctness first. The local model then iterates through progressively more optimized versions: fp8 KV (C3), MFMA outer products (C4), and chiplet-aware scheduling (C5).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleGetFunction({kernel_name}) failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    return module, func\n\n\ndef _launch(func, grid, block, shared_mem, *args):\n    arg_ptrs = []\n    for a in args:\n        arg_ptrs.append(ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(a), ctypes.c_void_p))\n    args_arr = (ctypes.c_void_p * len(arg_ptrs))(*arg_ptrs)\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleLaunchKernel(\n        func, grid[0], grid[1], grid[2],\n        block[0], block[1], block[2],\n        shared_mem, None, args_arr, None,\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleLaunchKernel failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n\n# ─── MLA Decode Kernel (C1: naive bf16 with online softmax) ─────────────\n\nMLA_KERNEL_SOURCE = r'''\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\n#include <hip/hip_bf16.h>\n\n/*\n * MLA Decode: Q[total_q, nheads, 576] @ KV[total_kv, 1, 576]^T -> softmax -> @V[:,:512]\n *\n * Grid:  (batch_size, nheads, 1)\n * Block: (BLOCK_SIZE, 1, 1)  where BLOCK_SIZE=256\n *\n * Each block handles one (batch, head) pair.\n * Threads cooperatively iterate over the KV sequence.\n * Online softmax: single pass computing running max + denominator.\n *\n * Memory layout:\n *   Q:   row-major [total_q, nheads, QK_DIM]  bf16\n *   KV:  row-major [total_kv, 1, QK_DIM]      bf16\n *   Out: row-major [total_q, nheads, V_DIM]    bf16\n */\n\n#define QK_DIM 576\n#define V_DIM 512\n#define BLOCK_SIZE 256\n\nextern \"C\" __global__\nvoid mla_decode_bf16(\n    __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ out,       // [total_q, nheads, V_DIM]\n    const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ q,   // [total_q, nheads, QK_DIM]\n    const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ kv,  // [total_kv, 1, QK_DIM]\n    const int* __restrict__ qo_indptr,      // [bs+1]\n    const int* __restrict__ kv_indptr,      // [bs+1]\n    int nheads,\n    float sm_scale\n) {\n    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;\n    int head_idx  = blockIdx.y;\n    int tid = threadIdx.x;\n\n    // Compute sequence boundaries\n    int q_start = qo_indptr[batch_idx];\n    int kv_start = kv_indptr[batch_idx];\n    int kv_end   = kv_indptr[batch_idx + 1];\n    int kv_len   = kv_end - kv_start;\n\n    // For decode: qseqlen=1, so q_idx = q_start\n    int q_idx = q_start;\n\n    // Load Q vector for this head into registers\n    // Each thread loads QK_DIM/BLOCK_SIZE elements\n    const int q_elements_per_thread = (QK_DIM + BLOCK_SIZE - 1) / BLOCK_SIZE;\n    float q_local[3];  // ceil(576/256) = 3\n    const __hip_bfloat16* q_row = q + (long long)q_idx * nheads * QK_DIM\n                                    + (long long)head_idx * QK_DIM;\n\n    for (int i = 0; i < q_elements_per_thread; i++) {\n        int col = tid + i * BLOCK_SIZE;\n        q_local[i] = (col < QK_DIM) ? __bfloat162float(q_row[col]) : 0.0f;\n    }\n\n    // Online softmax accumulators (per thread)\n    float thread_max = -1e20f;\n    float thread_sum = 0.0f;\n\n    // Value accumulator: each thread accumulates V_DIM/BLOCK_SIZE elements\n    const int v_elements_per_thread = (V_DIM + BLOCK_SIZE - 1) / BLOCK_SIZE;\n    float v_acc[2];  // ceil(512/256) = 2\n    for (int i = 0; i < v_elements_per_thread; i++) v_acc[i] = 0.0f;\n\n    // Shared memory for broadcasting dot product results\n    __shared__ float s_score[1];  // broadcast single score\n\n    // Iterate over KV sequence\n    for (int kv_pos = 0; kv_pos < kv_len; kv_pos++) {\n        const __hip_bfloat16* kv_row = kv + (long long)(kv_start + kv_pos) * QK_DIM;\n\n        // Step 1: Compute Q @ K^T (dot product) — parallel reduction\n        float dot = 0.0f;\n        for (int i = 0; i < q_elements_per_thread; i++) {\n            int col = tid + i * BLOCK_SIZE;\n            if (col < QK_DIM) {\n                float kv_val = __bfloat162float(kv_row[col]);\n                dot += q_local[i] * kv_val;\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Warp-level reduction for dot product\n        for (int offset = 32; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {\n            dot += __shfl_down(dot, offset);\n        }\n\n        // Cross-warp reduction via shared memory\n        __shared__ float s_partial[BLOCK_SIZE / 64];\n        if ((tid & 63) == 0) {\n            s_partial[tid / 64] = dot;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        // Final reduction by thread 0\n        if (tid == 0) {\n            float total = 0.0f;\n            for (int w = 0; w < BLOCK_SIZE / 64; w++) {\n                total += s_partial[w];\n            }\n            s_score[0] = total * sm_scale;\n        }\n        __syncthreads();\n\n        float score = s_score[0];\n\n        // Step 2: Online softmax update\n        float old_max = thread_max;\n        float new_max = fmaxf(old_max, score);\n        float exp_score = expf(score - new_max);\n        float correction = expf(old_max - new_max);\n\n        thread_sum = thread_sum * correction + exp_score;\n        thread_max = new_max;\n\n        // Step 3: Accumulate value (with online softmax correction)\n        for (int i = 0; i < v_elements_per_thread; i++) {\n            int col = tid + i * BLOCK_SIZE;\n            if (col < V_DIM) {\n                float v_val = __bfloat162float(kv_row[col]);\n                v_acc[i] = v_acc[i] * correction + exp_score * v_val;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Normalize by softmax denominator and write output\n    __hip_bfloat16* out_row = out + (long long)q_idx * nheads * V_DIM\n                                  + (long long)head_idx * V_DIM;\n\n    float inv_sum = (thread_sum > 0.0f) ? (1.0f / thread_sum) : 0.0f;\n    for (int i = 0; i < v_elements_per_thread; i++) {\n        int col = tid + i * BLOCK_SIZE;\n        if (col < V_DIM) {\n            out_row[col] = __float2bfloat16(v_acc[i] * inv_sum);\n        }\n    }\n}\n'''\n\n# ─── Fallback: aiter-based MLA ──────────────────────────────────────────\n\n_aiter_available = True\n\n\ndef _aiter_fallback(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"Fallback to current best aiter-based implementation.\"\"\"\n    from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd\n    from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes\n    from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1\n\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n    qseqlen = config[\"q_seq_len\"]\n    nheads = config[\"num_heads\"]\n\n    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen\n    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data[\"fp8\"]\n    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], 1, 1, kv_fp8.shape[-1])\n\n    # a16w8: bf16 Q + fp8 KV (skip Q quantization overhead)\n    q_input = q\n    q_scale = None\n    q_dtype = torch.bfloat16\n    kv_dtype = aiter_dtypes.fp8\n\n    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)\n\n    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(\n        bs, qseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype,\n        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=True,\n        num_kv_splits=32, intra_batch_mode=True,\n    )\n    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [\n        torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device=\"cuda\") for s, t in info\n    ]\n    get_mla_metadata_v1(\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,\n        nheads, 1, True,\n        wm, wis, wi, ri, rfm, rpm,\n        page_size=1, kv_granularity=16,\n        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen, uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,\n        fast_mode=True, max_split_per_batch=32,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        dtype_q=q_dtype, dtype_kv=kv_dtype,\n    )\n\n    o = torch.empty(\n        (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),\n        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\",\n    )\n\n    mla_decode_fwd(\n        q_input.view(-1, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM), kv_4d, o,\n        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_indices, kv_last_page_len,\n        qseqlen, page_size=1, nhead_kv=1,\n        sm_scale=SM_SCALE, logit_cap=0.0, num_kv_splits=32,\n        q_scale=q_scale, kv_scale=kv_scale,\n        intra_batch_mode=True,\n        work_meta_data=wm, work_indptr=wi, work_info_set=wis,\n        reduce_indptr=ri, reduce_final_map=rfm, reduce_partial_map=rpm,\n    )\n    return o\n\n\n# ─── Submission entry point ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n_hiprtc_ready = None\n\n\ndef _ensure_compiled():\n    \"\"\"Compile MLA kernel once, cache for subsequent calls.\"\"\"\n    global _mla_module, _mla_func, _hiprtc_ready\n\n    if _hiprtc_ready is not None:\n        return _hiprtc_ready\n\n    try:\n        if not _load_hip_libs():\n            print(\"[MLA-hiprtc] hiprtc not available, using aiter fallback\")\n            _hiprtc_ready = False\n            return False\n\n        _mla_module, _mla_func = _compile_kernel(MLA_KERNEL_SOURCE, \"mla_decode_bf16\")\n        _hiprtc_ready = True\n        print(\"[MLA-hiprtc] Kernel compiled successfully for gfx950\")\n        return True\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"[MLA-hiprtc] Compilation failed: {e}\")\n        _hiprtc_ready = False\n        return False\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"MLA decode via hiprtc (Phase C) with aiter fallback.\"\"\"\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data\n    bs = config[\"batch_size\"]\n    nheads = config[\"num_heads\"]\n    kvseqlen = config[\"kv_seq_len\"]\n\n    if not _ensure_compiled():\n        return _aiter_fallback(data)\n\n    # Use bf16 KV for the hiprtc kernel (C1: naive bf16)\n    kv_bf16 = kv_data[\"bf16\"]\n\n    # Allocate output\n    o = torch.empty(\n        (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),\n        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\",\n    )\n\n    # Launch: grid=(bs, nheads), block=(256)\n    _launch(\n        _mla_func,\n        (bs, nheads, 1), (256, 1, 1),\n        4 * (256 // 64 + 1),  # shared memory for partial sums + score\n        ctypes.c_void_p(o.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(q.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(kv_bf16.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(qo_indptr.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(kv_indptr.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_int(nheads),\n        ctypes.c_float(SM_SCALE),\n    )\n\n    return o\n"
```json
}
```

```
     1→"""
     2→FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
     3→Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).
     4→"""
     5→import torch
     6→from task import input_t, output_t
     7→from utils import make_match_reference
     8→from aiter import QuantType,dtypes
     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    11→# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
    12→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32
    13→
    14→def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):# -> input_t:
    15→    """
    16→    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.
    17→
    18→    Returns:
    19→        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    20→    """
    21→    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    22→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    23→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    24→    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    25→    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    26→    
    27→    # quantized mxfp4 B
    28→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    29→    B_q, B_scale_sh = quant_func(B, shuffle=True)
    30→    
    31→    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    32→    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    33→    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    34→
    35→def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    36→    x: torch.Tensor,
    37→    w: torch.Tensor,
    38→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    39→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    40→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
    41→) -> torch.Tensor:
    42→    """
    43→    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    44→    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    45→    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    46→    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    47→    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    48→    """
    49→    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    50→
    51→    m, _ = x.shape
    52→    n, _ = w.shape
    53→    # fp4 packed -> f32
    54→    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    55→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    56→    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    57→    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    58→    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    59→    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    60→    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    61→    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    62→    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    63→    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]
    64→
    65→
    66→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    67→    """
    68→    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    69→    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    70→    """
    71→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    72→    A = A.contiguous()
    73→    B = B.contiguous()
    74→    m, k = A.shape
    75→    n, _ = B.shape
    76→    
    77→    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    78→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    79→    # quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    80→    # quant_func(x, shuffle=False) -> (dtypes.fp4x2, scale); scale layout matches gemm_a4w4
    81→    # A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=False)
    82→    # B_q, B_scale = quant_func(B, shuffle=False)
    83→
    84→    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    85→    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    86→    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    87→    
    88→    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    89→    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
    90→    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
    91→    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)
    92→
    93→    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
    94→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    95→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    96→    A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    97→    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
    98→    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    99→        A_q,
   100→        B_shuffle,
   101→        A_scale_sh,
   102→        B_scale_sh,
   103→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
   104→        bpreshuffle=True,
   105→    )
   106→    return out_gemm
   107→
   108→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The GEMM pipeline has **two kernel launches**: (1) `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` quantizes A from bf16 to MXFP4 (block-32 with E8M0 scales), then (2) `gemm_a4w4` does the actual 4-bit GEMM with shuffled weights. Fusing these into a single hiprtc kernel eliminates the inter-kernel launch overhead (~10µs).
- **MXFP4 quantization** uses block size 32: for each 32-element group, find the max exponent (E8M0 = 8-bit exponent, no mantissa), then quantize each value to 4-bit E2M1 (2 exponent bits + 1 mantissa bit). Two FP4 values pack into one byte (fp4x2 format).
- The `shuffle_weight` + `bpreshuffle=True` pattern is a memory layout optimization for the HIP ASM GEMM kernel — it rearranges weight tiles to match the (16,16) layout expected by MFMA instructions. A fused hiprtc kernel would need to replicate this tiling.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```json
  "content": "\"\"\"\nPhase D: Fused MXFP4 quant + GEMM via hiprtc.\n\nFuses dynamic_mxfp4_quant + gemm_a4w4 into a single hiprtc kernel to\neliminate the two-kernel launch overhead (~10 us). The kernel:\n  1. Quantizes A tiles to MXFP4 on-the-fly in shared memory\n  2. Loads pre-shuffled B tiles (already in MXFP4)\n  3. Computes GEMM using MFMA intrinsics\n\nFalls back to the current two-kernel pipeline if hiprtc is unavailable.\n\nTEMPLATE for local model iteration — Claude writes spec, local model\ngenerates the optimized HIP C++ kernel body.\n\"\"\"\nimport ctypes\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\n\n# ─── hiprtc infrastructure (shared with MLA kernel) ─────────────────────\n\n_hiprtc_lib = None\n_hip_lib = None\n_gemm_module = None\n_gemm_func = None\n\n\ndef _load_hip_libs():\n    global _hiprtc_lib, _hip_lib\n    if _hiprtc_lib is not None:\n        return True\n    for name in [\"libhiprtc.so\", \"libhiprtc.so.6\", \"libhiprtc.so.5\"]:\n        try:\n            _hiprtc_lib = ctypes.CDLL(name)\n            break\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n    for name in [\"libamdhip64.so\", \"libamdhip64.so.6\", \"libamdhip64.so.5\"]:\n        try:\n            _hip_lib = ctypes.CDLL(name)\n            break\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n    return _hiprtc_lib is not None and _hip_lib is not None\n\n\ndef _compile_kernel(source: str, kernel_name: str, arch: str = \"gfx950\"):\n    prog = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCreateProgram(\n        ctypes.byref(prog), source.encode(), b\"gemm.hip\", 0, None, None,\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hiprtcCreateProgram failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    opts = [f\"--gpu-architecture={arch}\".encode()]\n    opts_arr = (ctypes.c_char_p * len(opts))(*opts)\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCompileProgram(prog, len(opts), opts_arr)\n    if rc != 0:\n        log_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetProgramLogSize(prog, ctypes.byref(log_size))\n        log = ctypes.create_string_buffer(log_size.value)\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetProgramLog(prog, log)\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"Compile failed:\\n{log.value.decode()}\")\n\n    code_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetCodeSize(prog, ctypes.byref(code_size))\n    code = ctypes.create_string_buffer(code_size.value)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetCode(prog, code)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n\n    module = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleLoadData(ctypes.byref(module), code)\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleLoadData failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    func = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleGetFunction(\n        ctypes.byref(func), module, kernel_name.encode(),\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleGetFunction({kernel_name}) failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    return module, func\n\n\ndef _launch(func, grid, block, shared_mem, *args):\n    arg_ptrs = []\n    for a in args:\n        arg_ptrs.append(ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(a), ctypes.c_void_p))\n    args_arr = (ctypes.c_void_p * len(arg_ptrs))(*arg_ptrs)\n    rc = _hip_lib.hipModuleLaunchKernel(\n        func, grid[0], grid[1], grid[2],\n        block[0], block[1], block[2],\n        shared_mem, None, args_arr, None,\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hipModuleLaunchKernel failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n\n# ─── Fused MXFP4 Quant + GEMM kernel ───────────────────────────────────\n\nFUSED_GEMM_SOURCE = r'''\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\n#include <hip/hip_bf16.h>\n\n/*\n * Fused MXFP4 Quant + GEMM\n *\n * A [M, K] bf16 — quantized on-the-fly to MXFP4\n * B [N, K] pre-quantized MXFP4 (shuffled, fp4x2 format)\n * B_scale [N_padded, K/32] E8M0 block scales (shuffled)\n * C [M, N] bf16 output\n *\n * Algorithm per tile (TILE_M x TILE_N):\n *   for each K-tile of size TILE_K:\n *     1. Load A tile [TILE_M, TILE_K] bf16 into shared memory\n *     2. Quantize A tile to MXFP4 in shared memory (block-32 E8M0)\n *     3. Load B tile [TILE_N, TILE_K] MXFP4 (pre-quantized)\n *     4. Dequantize both to f32 and compute MFMA outer product\n *     5. Accumulate in f32 registers\n *   Write C tile as bf16\n *\n * Grid:  (ceil(M/TILE_M), ceil(N/TILE_N), 1)\n * Block: (256, 1, 1)\n *\n * MXFP4 format: 2 FP4 (E2M1) values packed per byte\n * E8M0 scale: 1 scale per 32 elements (exponent only, no mantissa)\n *\n * NOTE: This is a NAIVE implementation for correctness validation.\n * The local finetuned model should iterate on:\n *   - MFMA intrinsics (mfma_f32_16x16x16_bf16 or mfma_f32_32x32x64_fp4)\n *   - LDS bank conflict avoidance\n *   - Double buffering for A/B tile loads\n *   - Optimal TILE_M/TILE_N/TILE_K for MI355X occupancy\n */\n\n#define TILE_M 64\n#define TILE_N 64\n#define TILE_K 32\n#define BLOCK_SIZE 256\n#define SCALE_GROUP 32\n\n// FP4 E2M1 lookup table (4-bit index -> float value)\n__device__ __constant__ float fp4_lut[16] = {\n    0.0f, 0.5f, 1.0f, 1.5f, 2.0f, 3.0f, 4.0f, 6.0f,\n    -0.0f, -0.5f, -1.0f, -1.5f, -2.0f, -3.0f, -4.0f, -6.0f\n};\n\n// E8M0 to float: scale = 2^(e8m0_val - 127)\n__device__ float e8m0_to_float(unsigned char e) {\n    int exp_val = (int)e - 127;\n    // Use ldexpf for exact power-of-2\n    return ldexpf(1.0f, exp_val);\n}\n\n// Dynamic MXFP4 quantize a block of 32 bf16 values\n// Returns packed fp4x2 (16 bytes) and E8M0 scale (1 byte)\n__device__ void quant_block_mxfp4(\n    const __hip_bfloat16* src,  // 32 bf16 values\n    unsigned char* dst_fp4,     // 16 bytes output (32 fp4 packed)\n    unsigned char* dst_scale    // 1 byte E8M0 scale\n) {\n    // Find max absolute value in block\n    float amax = 0.0f;\n    float vals[32];\n    for (int i = 0; i < 32; i++) {\n        vals[i] = __bfloat162float(src[i]);\n        float av = fabsf(vals[i]);\n        amax = fmaxf(amax, av);\n    }\n\n    // Compute E8M0 scale: find exponent such that max maps to FP4 range [0,6]\n    int exp_val;\n    if (amax < 1e-30f) {\n        exp_val = 0;\n    } else {\n        // FP4 E2M1 max = 6.0, so scale = amax / 6.0\n        // E8M0 = floor(log2(scale)) + 127\n        float scale = amax / 6.0f;\n        int e = 0;\n        frexpf(scale, &e);  // e = exponent\n        exp_val = e + 127 - 1;\n        if (exp_val < 0) exp_val = 0;\n        if (exp_val > 255) exp_val = 255;\n    }\n    *dst_scale = (unsigned char)exp_val;\n    float inv_scale = 1.0f / ldexpf(1.0f, exp_val - 127);\n\n    // Quantize each value to FP4 E2M1 and pack pairs\n    for (int i = 0; i < 16; i++) {\n        float v0 = vals[2*i] * inv_scale;\n        float v1 = vals[2*i+1] * inv_scale;\n\n        // Clamp and round to nearest FP4 value\n        // FP4 E2M1: {0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6} x {+,-}\n        auto quantize_fp4 = [](float v) -> unsigned char {\n            int sign = (v < 0.0f) ? 8 : 0;\n            float av = fabsf(v);\n            unsigned char idx;\n            if (av < 0.25f) idx = 0;\n            else if (av < 0.75f) idx = 1;\n            else if (av < 1.25f) idx = 2;\n            else if (av < 1.75f) idx = 3;\n            else if (av < 2.5f) idx = 4;\n            else if (av < 3.5f) idx = 5;\n            else if (av < 5.0f) idx = 6;\n            else idx = 7;\n            return (unsigned char)(sign | idx);\n        };\n\n        unsigned char q0 = quantize_fp4(v0);\n        unsigned char q1 = quantize_fp4(v1);\n        dst_fp4[i] = (q1 << 4) | q0;  // pack two fp4 into one byte\n    }\n}\n\nextern \"C\" __global__\nvoid fused_mxfp4_gemm(\n    __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ C,          // [M, N] output\n    const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ A,    // [M, K] input\n    const unsigned char* __restrict__ B_q,   // [N, K/2] pre-quantized fp4x2\n    const unsigned char* __restrict__ B_scale, // [N_pad, K/32] E8M0 scales\n    int M, int N, int K,\n    int N_pad  // padded N for B_scale stride\n) {\n    int tile_m = blockIdx.x;\n    int tile_n = blockIdx.y;\n    int tid = threadIdx.x;\n\n    int row_start = tile_m * TILE_M;\n    int col_start = tile_n * TILE_N;\n\n    // Each thread accumulates a subset of the TILE_M x TILE_N output\n    // Thread layout: TILE_M * TILE_N / BLOCK_SIZE elements per thread\n    const int elems_per_thread = (TILE_M * TILE_N) / BLOCK_SIZE;  // 64*64/256 = 16\n    float acc[16];\n    for (int i = 0; i < 16; i++) acc[i] = 0.0f;\n\n    // Iterate over K dimension in TILE_K chunks\n    for (int k_tile = 0; k_tile < K; k_tile += TILE_K) {\n        // Each thread loads and processes elements\n        for (int e = 0; e < elems_per_thread; e++) {\n            int flat_idx = tid + e * BLOCK_SIZE;\n            int local_m = flat_idx / TILE_N;\n            int local_n = flat_idx % TILE_N;\n            int global_m = row_start + local_m;\n            int global_n = col_start + local_n;\n\n            if (global_m >= M || global_n >= N) continue;\n\n            float dot = 0.0f;\n            for (int dk = 0; dk < TILE_K && (k_tile + dk) < K; dk++) {\n                int global_k = k_tile + dk;\n\n                // A value (bf16)\n                float a_val = __bfloat162float(\n                    A[(long long)global_m * K + global_k]\n                );\n\n                // B value (dequantize from MXFP4)\n                int b_byte_idx = global_n * (K / 2) + global_k / 2;\n                unsigned char b_packed = B_q[b_byte_idx];\n                unsigned char b_nibble;\n                if (global_k % 2 == 0) {\n                    b_nibble = b_packed & 0x0F;\n                } else {\n                    b_nibble = (b_packed >> 4) & 0x0F;\n                }\n                float b_val = fp4_lut[b_nibble];\n\n                // B scale (E8M0)\n                int scale_idx = global_n * (K / SCALE_GROUP) + global_k / SCALE_GROUP;\n                // Use N_pad stride for scale array\n                int scale_row = global_n;\n                int scale_col = global_k / SCALE_GROUP;\n                unsigned char b_e8m0 = B_scale[\n                    (long long)scale_row * (K / SCALE_GROUP) + scale_col\n                ];\n                float b_scale_val = e8m0_to_float(b_e8m0);\n                b_val *= b_scale_val;\n\n                dot += a_val * b_val;\n            }\n\n            acc[e] += dot;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Write output\n    for (int e = 0; e < elems_per_thread; e++) {\n        int flat_idx = tid + e * BLOCK_SIZE;\n        int local_m = flat_idx / TILE_N;\n        int local_n = flat_idx % TILE_N;\n        int global_m = row_start + local_m;\n        int global_n = col_start + local_n;\n\n        if (global_m < M && global_n < N) {\n            C[(long long)global_m * N + global_n] = __float2bfloat16(acc[e]);\n        }\n    }\n}\n'''\n\n# ─── Fallback: two-kernel pipeline ──────────────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef _two_kernel_fallback(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"Current best: dynamic_mxfp4_quant + gemm_a4w4.\"\"\"\n    from aiter import dtypes\n    import aiter\n    from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_mxfp4_quant\n    from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import e8m0_shuffle\n\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n    x_fp4, bs_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A.contiguous())\n    A_q = x_fp4.view(dtypes.fp4x2)\n    A_scale_sh = e8m0_shuffle(bs_e8m0).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)\n    return aiter.gemm_a4w4(\n        A_q, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,\n        dtype=dtypes.bf16, bpreshuffle=True,\n    )\n\n\n# ─── Submission entry point ─────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n_hiprtc_ready = None\n\n\ndef _ensure_compiled():\n    global _gemm_module, _gemm_func, _hiprtc_ready\n\n    if _hiprtc_ready is not None:\n        return _hiprtc_ready\n\n    try:\n        if not _load_hip_libs():\n            print(\"[GEMM-hiprtc] hiprtc not available, using two-kernel fallback\")\n            _hiprtc_ready = False\n            return False\n\n        _gemm_module, _gemm_func = _compile_kernel(\n            FUSED_GEMM_SOURCE, \"fused_mxfp4_gemm\",\n        )\n        _hiprtc_ready = True\n        print(\"[GEMM-hiprtc] Fused kernel compiled successfully for gfx950\")\n        return True\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"[GEMM-hiprtc] Compilation failed: {e}\")\n        _hiprtc_ready = False\n        return False\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    \"\"\"Fused MXFP4 quant + GEMM via hiprtc with two-kernel fallback.\"\"\"\n    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data\n\n    if not _ensure_compiled():\n        return _two_kernel_fallback(data)\n\n    M, K = A.shape\n    N = B.shape[0]\n\n    # Allocate output\n    C = torch.empty((M, N), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=\"cuda\")\n\n    # B_q is pre-quantized fp4x2 [N, K/2]\n    # B_scale_sh is shuffled E8M0 scales\n    # For the naive hiprtc kernel, we need unshuffled B and scales\n    # NOTE: The naive kernel uses B_q directly (not shuffled) and\n    # B_scale from the original quantization.\n    # For the hiprtc path, we re-quantize B to get unshuffled format.\n    # This is a correctness-first approach — the local model will\n    # optimize to use pre-shuffled formats matching MFMA tile layout.\n\n    # Use B_q (unshuffled) and compute scales from B\n    from aiter import QuantType\n    import aiter\n    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)\n    B_q_unshuffle, B_scale_unshuffle = quant_func(B, shuffle=False)\n\n    # Get raw bytes for ctypes\n    B_q_bytes = B_q_unshuffle.view(torch.uint8)\n    B_scale_bytes = B_scale_unshuffle.view(torch.uint8)\n\n    N_pad = B_scale_bytes.shape[0]\n\n    # Grid: (ceil(M/64), ceil(N/64))\n    grid_m = (M + 63) // 64\n    grid_n = (N + 63) // 64\n\n    _launch(\n        _gemm_func,\n        (grid_m, grid_n, 1), (256, 1, 1),\n        0,  # shared memory (naive version uses none)\n        ctypes.c_void_p(C.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(A.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(B_q_bytes.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_void_p(B_scale_bytes.data_ptr()),\n        ctypes.c_int(M),\n        ctypes.c_int(N),\n        ctypes.c_int(K),\n        ctypes.c_int(N_pad),\n    )\n\n    return C\n"
}
```

---

## Implementation Complete: Phase 12 — hiprtc + Local Model Delegation

### Files Created (6 files, 1,969 LOC)

| File | LOC | Phase | Purpose |
|------|-----|-------|---------|
| `src/cohezion/skills/HIP_KERNEL_ENGINEER_PRIME.md` | 212 | 0D | PRIME skill for local model kernel delegation |
| `scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py` | 416 | 0A-0C | Full LoRA finetune pipeline (collect→train→export→deploy) |
| `kernels/mixed-mla/submission_probe_hiprtc.py` | 253 | A | Environment probe (hip-python, ctypes, hipcc, torch arch) |
| `kernels/mixed-mla/submission_hiprtc_validate.py` | 304 | B | Pipeline validation (vector copy + bf16 dot product) |
| `kernels/mixed-mla/submission_hiprtc_mla.py` | 378 | C | MLA decode kernel (naive bf16, online softmax) |
| `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_hiprtc_fused.py` | 406 | D | Fused MXFP4 quant + GEMM kernel |

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **All submissions follow a graceful fallback pattern**: if hiprtc isn't available on the runner, each submission falls back to the current best aiter-based implementation. This means no submission can regress — the probe (Phase A) just determines which optimization path is available.
- **The hiprtc compile→load→launch pattern** is shared across all kernel submissions via a consistent `_load_hip_libs()` / `_compile_kernel()` / `_launch()` helper trio. This uses `ctypes.CDLL` to load `libhiprtc.so` and `libamdhip64.so`, compile HIP C++ source strings, and launch kernels via `hipModuleLaunchKernel`.
- **The finetune script** is a 4-stage pipeline designed to run entirely on the Strix Halo (128GB RAM) with zero Claude token cost: `collect` gathers HIP/CUDA kernel source from ThunderKittens, ROCm examples, aiter, and our own 11 phases of submissions; `train` runs QLoRA with Unsloth; `export` converts to GGUF; `deploy` registers with Ollama as `hip-kernel-dev`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### Execution Order

```
Phase 0: python scripts/finetune_kernel_model.py all     (LOCAL, ~2h)
Phase A: popcorn-cli submit --mode test ... submission_probe_hiprtc.py
         → Decision gate: hiprtc available?
Phase B: popcorn-cli submit --mode test ... submission_hiprtc_validate.py
         → Decision gate: dispatch < 10µs?
Phase C: popcorn-cli submit --mode test ... submission_hiprtc_mla.py
         → Local model iterates: C1→C2→C3→C4→C5
Phase D: popcorn-cli submit --mode test ... submission_hiprtc_fused.py
         → Local model iterates on MFMA optimization
```

### What the Local Model Iterates On

The C1 (naive bf16) and fused GEMM kernels are **correctness-first templates**. The local finetuned model generates optimized variants following the refinement ladder:

- **MLA**: bf16 naive → bf16 online softmax → fp8 KV → MFMA outer products → chiplet-aware scheduling
- **GEMM**: naive element-wise → shared memory tiling → MFMA `mfma_f32_32x32x64_fp4` → double buffering

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/.claude/skills/hiprtc-ctypes-kernel-launch/SKILL.md",
  "content": "---\nname: hiprtc-ctypes-kernel-launch\ndescription: |\n  Pattern for compiling and launching HIP C++ GPU kernels from Python via\n  ctypes without hip-python. Use when: (1) building AMD GPU kernels that\n  bypass Python dispatch overhead, (2) hip-python package is unavailable,\n  (3) embedding HIP C++ kernel source strings in submission.py for hiprtc\n  runtime compilation on gfx950/MI355X. Key insight: ctypes loads libhiprtc.so\n  + libamdhip64.so directly, passing PyTorch tensor .data_ptr() as c_void_p.\n  Enables <10µs kernel dispatch vs ~130µs Python-level aiter dispatch.\nauthor: Claude Code\nversion: 1.0.0\n---\n\n# hiprtc ctypes Kernel Launch\n\n## Problem\n\nAMD MI355X runners may not have the `hip-python` package installed, but\n`libhiprtc.so` and `libamdhip64.so` are available. The hip-python API is\nalso version-sensitive. ctypes provides a stable alternative with no package\ndependency — load the shared libraries directly and call the C API.\n\nThe key challenge: `hipModuleLaunchKernel` requires a `void**` array where\neach element points to the actual argument. PyTorch tensor data pointers\nmust be wrapped as `ctypes.c_void_p` and then addressed with\n`ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(arg), ctypes.c_void_p)`.\n\n## The ctypes API Sequence\n\n```\nhiprtcCreateProgram → hiprtcCompileProgram → hiprtcGetCode →\nhipModuleLoadData → hipModuleGetFunction → hipModuleLaunchKernel\n```\n\n## Solution\n\n### 1. Load Libraries\n\n```python\nimport ctypes\n\n_hiprtc_lib = None\n_hip_lib = None\n\ndef _load_hip_libs():\n    global _hiprtc_lib, _hip_lib\n    if _hiprtc_lib is not None:\n        return True\n    for name in [\"libhiprtc.so\", \"libhiprtc.so.6\", \"libhiprtc.so.5\"]:\n        try:\n            _hiprtc_lib = ctypes.CDLL(name)\n            break\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n    for name in [\"libamdhip64.so\", \"libamdhip64.so.6\", \"libamdhip64.so.5\"]:\n        try:\n            _hip_lib = ctypes.CDLL(name)\n            break\n        except OSError:\n            continue\n    return _hiprtc_lib is not None and _hip_lib is not None\n```\n\n### 2. Compile HIP C++ Source String\n\n```python\ndef _compile_kernel(source: str, kernel_name: str, arch: str = \"gfx950\"):\n    \"\"\"Returns (module, func) handles. Cache these — recompile is expensive.\"\"\"\n    prog = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCreateProgram(\n        ctypes.byref(prog),\n        source.encode(), b\"kernel.hip\",\n        0, None, None,           # num_headers, headers, header_names\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"hiprtcCreateProgram failed: rc={rc}\")\n\n    opts = [f\"--gpu-architecture={arch}\".encode()]\n    opts_arr = (ctypes.c_char_p * len(opts))(*opts)\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCompileProgram(prog, len(opts), opts_arr)\n    if rc != 0:\n        # Get compile log for debugging\n        log_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetProgramLogSize(prog, ctypes.byref(log_size))\n        log = ctypes.create_string_buffer(log_size.value)\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetProgramLog(prog, log)\n        _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n        raise RuntimeError(f\"Compile failed:\\n{log.value.decode()}\")\n\n    # Get compiled code\n    code_size = ctypes.c_size_t()\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetCodeSize(prog, ctypes.byref(code_size))\n    code = ctypes.create_string_buffer(code_size.value)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcGetCode(prog, code)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n\n    # Load module and get function\n    module = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    _hip_lib.hipModuleLoadData(ctypes.byref(module), code)\n    func = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    _hip_lib.hipModuleGetFunction(\n        ctypes.byref(func), module, kernel_name.encode(),\n    )\n    return module, func\n```\n\n### 3. Launch Kernel (Critical: void** args pattern)\n\n```python\ndef _launch(func, grid, block, shared_mem, *args):\n    \"\"\"\n    grid  = (gx, gy, gz)\n    block = (bx, by, bz)\n    args  = mix of ctypes values: c_void_p, c_int, c_float, etc.\n\n    Each arg must be a ctypes value; its ADDRESS is passed in the void** array.\n    \"\"\"\n    arg_ptrs = []\n    for a in args:\n        arg_ptrs.append(ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(a), ctypes.c_void_p))\n    args_arr = (ctypes.c_void_p * len(arg_ptrs))(*arg_ptrs)\n\n    _hip_lib.hipModuleLaunchKernel(\n        func,\n        grid[0], grid[1], grid[2],     # gridDimX, Y, Z\n        block[0], block[1], block[2],   # blockDimX, Y, Z\n        shared_mem,                     # sharedMemBytes\n        None,                           # stream (default stream)\n        args_arr,                       # kernelParams\n        None,                           # extra\n    )\n```\n\n### 4. Passing PyTorch Tensors\n\n```python\n# Correct: wrap data_ptr() as c_void_p, pass by address\nout = torch.empty(N, dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\ninp = torch.randn(N, dtype=torch.float32, device=\"cuda\")\n\n_launch(\n    func,\n    (grid_x, 1, 1), (block_x, 1, 1), 0,\n    ctypes.c_void_p(out.data_ptr()),  # float* dst\n    ctypes.c_void_p(inp.data_ptr()),  # const float* src\n    ctypes.c_int(N),                  # int N\n)\n```\n\n### 5. Kernel Source Requirements\n\nKernels compiled with hiprtc must use `extern \"C\"` linkage:\n\n```cpp\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\n\n// REQUIRED: extern \"C\" so hipModuleGetFunction can find it by name\nextern \"C\" __global__\nvoid my_kernel(float* dst, const float* src, int N) {\n    int idx = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n    if (idx < N) dst[idx] = src[idx];\n}\n```\n\nWithout `extern \"C\"`, the function name is C++ mangled and\n`hipModuleGetFunction` will fail with rc != 0.\n\n## Environment Probe Pattern\n\nBefore using hiprtc in a submission, probe availability:\n\n```python\ndef _check_hiprtc():\n    \"\"\"Returns True if hiprtc compile pipeline is functional.\"\"\"\n    if not _load_hip_libs():\n        return False\n\n    test_source = b'extern \"C\" __global__ void noop() {}'\n    prog = ctypes.c_void_p()\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCreateProgram(\n        ctypes.byref(prog), test_source, b\"t.hip\", 0, None, None,\n    )\n    if rc != 0:\n        return False\n\n    opts = [b\"--gpu-architecture=gfx950\"]\n    opts_arr = (ctypes.c_char_p * 1)(*opts)\n    rc = _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcCompileProgram(prog, 1, opts_arr)\n    _hiprtc_lib.hiprtcDestroyProgram(ctypes.byref(prog))\n    return rc == 0\n```\n\n## Submission.py Wrapper Pattern\n\n```python\n_module = None\n_func = None\n_hiprtc_ready = None\n\ndef _ensure_compiled():\n    global _module, _func, _hiprtc_ready\n    if _hiprtc_ready is not None:\n        return _hiprtc_ready\n    try:\n        _module, _func = _compile_kernel(KERNEL_SOURCE, \"kernel_name\")\n        _hiprtc_ready = True\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"[hiprtc] Compilation failed: {e}\")\n        _hiprtc_ready = False\n    return _hiprtc_ready\n\ndef custom_kernel(data):\n    if not _ensure_compiled():\n        return ref_kernel(data)  # graceful fallback\n    # ... unpack data, launch kernel ...\n```\n\n## Known Issues\n\n| Issue | Cause | Fix |\n|-------|-------|-----|\n| `hipModuleGetFunction` rc != 0 | Missing `extern \"C\"` | Add `extern \"C\"` to kernel |\n| `hiprtcCompileProgram` rc != 0 | Wrong arch flag | Check `--gpu-architecture=gfx950` |\n| `OSError: libhiprtc.so` | Library not in LD_LIBRARY_PATH | Try `.so.6` or `.so.5` variants |\n| Wrong kernel results | c_int vs c_long mismatch | Match ctypes type exactly to kernel parameter type |\n\n## BF16 Header for hip_runtime.h\n\nFor bf16 kernels, include the extra header:\n\n```cpp\n#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>\n#include <hip/hip_bf16.h>\n\n// bf16 values: __hip_bfloat16\n// Conversions: __bfloat162float, __float2bfloat16\n```\n\n## Performance Context\n\n| Method | Dispatch Overhead |\n|--------|------------------|\n| Python aiter (Triton JIT) | ~130 µs |\n| hiprtc ctypes (cached) | ~5–10 µs |\n| hiprtc ctypes (first call) | ~1–5 s (compilation) |\n| AMD competition leader | ~4.3 µs |\n\nCompile once, cache `(module, func)` globally. All subsequent calls pay\nonly the ctypes overhead (~5–10 µs).\n\n## Related Skills\n\n- `popcorn-cli-amd-kernel-submission` — submission workflow\n- `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla` — VGPR register budget\n- `amd-gfx950-tl-dot-scaled-constraints` — MFMA constraints\n"
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The most extractable pattern from this session was the **`void**` kernel args pattern** in `hipModuleLaunchKernel` — it's the exact point where Python developers get stuck. Each argument's *address* (not value) goes into the array, which means `ctypes.cast(ctypes.pointer(arg), ctypes.c_void_p)`. This double-indirection is non-obvious and not well-documented outside of NVIDIA's CUDA documentation.
- The **`extern "C"` requirement** for hiprtc is critical but easily missed: without it, C++ name mangling makes `hipModuleGetFunction` fail with a cryptic non-zero return code. The skill documents this as the #1 gotcha.
- The 256-line skill file follows the project pattern of keeping skills under 300 lines by referencing related skills (`popcorn-cli-amd-kernel-submission`, `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla`) rather than duplicating their content.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

I see the critical new information from the skill update. The competition runner **blocks hiprtc via static source code scanning** — any submission containing `hipModuleLaunchKernel`, `hipModuleLoadData`, etc. gets a misleading `500 Internal Server Error` about "work on another stream."

This fundamentally changes the plan. Let me assess the impact and refine.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **hiprtc is a dead end for Popcorn CLI competitions.** The runner performs static source scanning before execution, rejecting any code that contains HIP module API strings. The error message ("Your code contains work on another stream") is misleading — it's not a runtime stream violation, it's a source-level ban on `hipModuleLaunchKernel` and related APIs.
- This means the ~130µs Python dispatch floor **cannot be bypassed** via runtime kernel compilation on these platforms. The 4.3µs leader must be achieving their results through a different mechanism entirely — likely they have custom Triton kernels or use aiter APIs that we haven't explored, or the benchmark harness gives them a different dispatch path.
- The `competitive-kernel-optimization-ceiling` skill likely has relevant guidance for this situation.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

This is extremely valuable context. The `competitive-kernel-optimization-ceiling` skill now includes Phase 12 findings confirming **hiprtc is permanently blocked** on Popcorn CLI. Let me internalize the full picture and refine the plan.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **The entire hiprtc approach is a dead end.** The competition runner performs static source scanning that rejects `hipModuleLaunchKernel`, `hipModuleLoadData`, `hipModuleGetFunction`, and `libamdhip64.so` strings — even inside dead code branches. The misleading "work on another stream" error masks the real cause.
- **The Python dispatch floor is ~20-25µs** — every torch op composition pays this. The 4.3µs MLA leader uses a single fused CK/ASM kernel with zero Python overhead. No Python-level approach can reach this.
- **Phase 11's three-regime routing with adaptive splits (69.5µs)** was the best-ever MLA submission. Phase 12's 3D matmul gained marginally but lost the threshold calibration, regressing to 79µs. The optimal path is combining Phase 11 calibration + Phase 12 3D matmul.
- **The finetune script and PRIME skill remain useful** — they support local model delegation for kernel development on platforms without source scanning, and for other GPU optimization work.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Given this, the hiprtc submission files need to be updated with clear warnings, and the real value now shifts to:

1. **Combining Phase 11 + Phase 12 best** for MLA (three-regime routing + 3D matmul + adaptive splits)
2. **Keeping the finetune infrastructure** for use on non-Popcorn platforms
3. **Updating the hiprtc submissions** with "BLOCKED" warnings so future sessions don't retry

```
     1→"""
     2→MLA decode: hybrid a16w8/a8w8 dtype routing with metadata caching.
     3→
     4→Key insight: a16w8 (bf16 Q + fp8 KV) eliminates Q quantization (~25 us)
     5→but doubles Q bandwidth. Optimal strategy:
     6→  - total_kv <= 262144: a16w8 (compute/launch overhead dominant)
     7→  - total_kv > 262144:  a8w8  (bandwidth dominant, fp8 Q halves load)
     8→
     9→Metadata cached per (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, dtype_mode).
    10→Fresh output buffer each call to avoid stale data on seed changes.
    11→"""
    12→import torch
    13→from task import input_t, output_t
    14→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    15→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    16→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    17→
    18→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576 ** 0.5)
    19→V_HEAD_DIM = 512
    20→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    21→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    22→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    23→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    24→BF16_DTYPE = torch.bfloat16
    25→
    26→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    27→A16W8_THRESHOLD = 262144
    28→
    29→_cache: dict = {}
    30→
    31→
    32→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    33→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    34→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    35→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    36→    return (
    37→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    38→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    39→    )
    40→
    41→
    42→def _build_cache(bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype, qo_indptr, kv_indptr):
    43→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    44→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    45→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
    46→
    47→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    48→        bs, qseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
    49→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=True,
    50→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS, intra_batch_mode=True,
    51→    )
    52→    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [
    53→        torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info
    54→    ]
    55→
    56→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    57→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
    58→        nheads // NUM_KV_HEADS, NUM_KV_HEADS, True,
    59→        wm, wis, wi, ri, rfm, rpm,
    60→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
    61→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
    62→        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen, uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
    63→        fast_mode=True,
    64→        max_split_per_batch=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
    65→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    66→        dtype_q=q_dtype, dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
    67→    )
    68→
    69→    return {
    70→        "kv_indices": kv_indices,
    71→        "kv_last_page_len": kv_last_page_len,
    72→        "work_meta_data": wm,
    73→        "work_indptr": wi,
    74→        "work_info_set": wis,
    75→        "reduce_indptr": ri,
    76→        "reduce_final_map": rfm,
    77→        "reduce_partial_map": rpm,
    78→    }
    79→
    80→
    81→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    82→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    83→    bs = config["batch_size"]
    84→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
    85→    qseqlen = config["q_seq_len"]
    86→    nheads = config["num_heads"]
    87→
    88→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    89→    use_a16w8 = total_kv <= A16W8_THRESHOLD
    90→
    91→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
    92→    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])
    93→
    94→    if use_a16w8:
    95→        q_input = q
    96→        q_scale = None
    97→        q_dtype = BF16_DTYPE
    98→    else:
    99→        q_input, q_scale = _quantize_fp8(q)
   100→        q_dtype = FP8_DTYPE
   101→
   102→    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, use_a16w8)
   103→    if key not in _cache:
   104→        _cache[key] = _build_cache(
   105→            bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads,
   106→            q_dtype, FP8_DTYPE,
   107→            qo_indptr, kv_indptr,
   108→        )
   109→    c = _cache[key]

   111→    o = torch.empty(

   113→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda",
   114→    )
   115→
   116→    mla_decode_fwd(
```

> AGENT

Good. Now I can see the current `submission.py` is missing the key Phase 11 wins:

1. **No einsum path** — it always uses aiter `mla_decode_fwd`, even for tiny batches where torch ops are 5x faster
2. **Fixed `NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32`** — Phase 11 showed adaptive splits (1/4/8/16/32) based on total_kv improves geomean
3. **No 3D matmul** — Phase 12 showed `torch.matmul` is 5-9% faster than `torch.einsum` for decode

The combined best needs all three. Let me create it.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The current submission uses aiter's `mla_decode_fwd` for ALL shapes. For small batches (bs=4, kv=1k), the 3-stage aiter pipeline has ~100-150µs of fixed overhead that dominates. `torch.matmul` dispatches to hipBLAS GEMV in ~25µs.
- Adaptive `num_kv_splits` matters because split-K parallelism trades launch overhead for parallelism. For small KV sequences (≤2k), even split=1 is fine — the sequence fits in one CU's workload. For large sequences (>500k), split=32 hides memory latency across multiple CUs.
- The 3D matmul trick: `q.view(bs, nheads, 576) @ kv.transpose(1,2)` is a true batched GEMM with no broadcasting, while `torch.einsum("bqnh,bsh->bnqs", ...)` parses a format string and may create intermediate views. For decode (qseqlen=1), the 3D path avoids the string overhead.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

savings).\n\nRegime 3 — aiter a8w8 (large workloads):\n  total_kv > A16W8_THRESHOLD\n  fp8 Q + fp8 KV: halves Q bandwidth for bandwidth-bound shapes.\n\nMetadata cached per (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, dtype_mode).\nAdaptive num_kv_splits based on total_kv size.\n\"\"\"\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd\nfrom aiter import dtypes as 262144\n\n_cache: dict = {}\n\n\ndef _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Adaptive split-K factor based on KV sequence length.\"\"\"\n    if total_kv <= 2048:\n        return 1\n    if total_kv <= 16384:\n        return 4\n    if total_kv <= 131072:\n        return 8\n    if total_kv <= 524288:\n        return 16\n    return 32\n\n\ndef _quantize_fp8(tensor):\n    finfo = kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device=\"cuda\")\n    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)\n\n    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(\n        bs, kvseqlen\n\n    # Regime 1: 3D matmul for small batches (bypass aiter pipeline overhead)\n    if bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS and total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV:\n        return _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads)\n\n    # Regime 2: a16w8 for medium (skip Q quantization)\n    # Regime 3: a8w8 for large (fp8 Q halves bandwidth)\n    use_a16w8 = total_kv <= A16W8_THRESHOLD\n    return _aiter_decode(\n        q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config,\n        bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv, use_a16w8,\n    )\n"
```json
}
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **Three-regime architecture** is the key pattern here: instead of one approach for all shapes, we use the optimal strategy per workload size. This is common in high-performance computing — different algorithms dominate at different scales.
- **The `_build_cache` now stores `num_kv_splits`** alongside the metadata tensors, so each shape gets its cached metadata computed with the correct split factor. The cache key includes `num_kv_splits` transitively via `use_a16w8` + the shape tuple.
- **`MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 131072`** (Phase 12's confirmed optimal) is higher than Phase 11's `65536`, meaning more shapes route to the fast matmul path. Phase 12 proved that bf16 matmul is still faster than aiter's fp8 pipeline up to 131k tokens because the pipeline overhead dominates.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Summary of Combined Best MLA Submission

The new `submission.py` merges the best discoveries from Phases 11 and 12:

| Feature | Previous (Phase 10) | Combined Best |
|---------|---------------------|---------------|
| Small-batch path | None (always aiter) | **3D `torch.matmul`** for bs≤4, total_kv≤131k |
| `num_kv_splits` | Fixed 32 | **Adaptive** 1/4/8/16/32 based on total_kv |
| Q dtype routing | a16w8 vs a8w8 at 262k | Same (proven optimal) |
| Metadata caching | Per (shape, dtype) | Same + includes `num_kv_splits` in key |
| Small-batch dispatch | `torch.einsum` (Phase 11) | **`torch.matmul` 3D** (5-9% faster, no string parsing) |

**Expected performance**: Phase 11 achieved 69.5µs geomean with einsum + adaptive splits. The 3D matmul should shave another 5-9% off the small-batch shapes, targeting ~65µs geomean.

**Next step**: Submit via `popcorn-cli --mode test` then `--mode benchmark` to validate correctness and measure improvement. This requires access to the MI355X runner.

   242→
   243→# Luma AMD Speedrun: Phase 9 — Team-Parallel Kernel Optimization
   244→
   245→## Context
   246→
   247→**Phase 8 is complete.** Three kernels are on the leaderboard. Significant gaps remain.
   248→
   249→| Kernel | Our Time | Leader | Gap | Current Approach | Bottleneck |
   250→|--------|----------|--------|-----|-----------------|------------|
   251→| MLA | ~129 µs | 4.3 µs | **30x** | FlashDecoding + hybrid routing (threshold=100k) | VGPR pressure (kv1[64,512]=256 VGPRs); bf16 bandwidth |
   252→| GEMM | ~20.6 µs | 9.7 µs | **2.1x** | `gemm_afp4wfp4` (Triton) | API ceiling; gemm_a4w4 blocked by callsite sensitivity |
   253→| MoE | ~155 µs | 145 µs | **1.07x** | Adaptive KSPLIT routing | API ceiling |
   254→
   255→**Phase 8 key learnings** (skills: `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla`, `amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness`):
   256→1. VGPR pressure from KV tile, not accumulator — BLOCK_M tiling didn't help
   257→2. BLOCK_M tiling increases KV bandwidth (nq/BLOCK_M independent KV loads)
   258→3. ref_kernel has ~130 µs fixed overhead (metadata + JIT dispatch)
   259→4. gemm_a4w4 HIP ASM produces ~1-3% different results from submission.py callsite
   260→5. For shapes under 100k tokens, kernel launch + Python overhead dominate
   261→
   262→**Why a team approach:** Phase 8 was sequential — one agent doing MLA, then GEMM, then MoE.
   263→The three kernels are independent (different files, leaderboards, and optimization strategies).
   264→A team can parallelize research and code writing while the lead handles sequential submissions.
   265→
   266→## Team Architecture
   267→
   268→**Team name:** `kernel-speedrun`
   269→
   270→| Agent | Role | File Ownership | Tools Needed |
   271→|-------|------|----------------|-------------|
   272→| **Lead** (main session) | Coordinates, submits via popcorn-cli, reviews results | None (reads all) | Bash (popcorn-cli), SendMessage |
   273→| **mla-dev** | MLA kernel research + implementation | `kernels/mixed-mla/` | Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Bash |
   274→| **gemm-dev** | GEMM kernel research + implementation | `kernels/mxfp4-mm/` | Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Bash |
   275→
   276→**Why 3 agents, not more:**
   277→- Only 2 kernels have meaningful improvement headroom (MoE is at 1.07x gap — diminishing returns)
   278→- Popcorn-cli submissions are sequential (~10 min each) — the lead is the bottleneck
   279→- More agents = more coordination overhead + linear token cost
   280→- 3 is the sweet spot: 2 implementers saturate the submission pipeline
   281→
   282→**Why not a dedicated research agent:**
   283→- Each kernel's research is highly specialized (MLA: attention algorithms; GEMM: quantized matmul)
   284→- A generalist researcher would duplicate context that kernel-specific agents already need
   285→- Each dev agent does targeted research within their domain
   286→
   287→### Communication Protocol
   288→
   289→```
   290→mla-dev ──writes code──→ kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py
   291→         ──messages────→ Lead: "ready for submission"
   292→
   293→gemm-dev ──writes code─→ kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py
   294→          ──messages───→ Lead: "ready for submission"
   295→
   296→Lead ──submits──→ popcorn-cli (sequential, ~10 min per submit)
   297→     ──messages─→ mla-dev/gemm-dev: "results: X µs, errors: Y"
   298→```
   299→
   300→**Submission queue:** Lead maintains a FIFO queue. While waiting for one submission,
   301→the other agent can be writing code. Typical cycle:
   302→1. mla-dev signals ready → Lead submits MLA → 10 min wait
   303→2. During wait, gemm-dev signals ready → Lead queues GEMM
   304→3. MLA result arrives → Lead messages mla-dev → Lead submits GEMM
   305→4. While GEMM runs, mla-dev iterates based on results
   306→
   307→## Files to Modify
   308→
   309→**Base path:** `.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/`
   310→
   311→| File | Owner | Kernel |
   312→|------|-------|--------|
   313→| `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py` | mla-dev | MLA |
   314→| `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` | gemm-dev | GEMM |
   315→
   316→**Reference (read-only, shared):**
   317→- `kernels/mixed-mla/reference.py` — aiter fp8 MLA decode, quantize_fp8, generate_input
   318→- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/reference.py` — aiter gemm_a4w4 reference
   319→- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_tritonblas_fused.py` — existing fused GEMM (264 lines, Origami bug)
   320→
   321→**Skills (both agents should read):**
   322→- `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla` — VGPR budget, BLOCK_M bandwidth tradeoff
   323→- `amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness` — gemm_a4w4 callsite sensitivity

   328→
   329→## Progress Tracking
   330→
   331→### MLA Track (mla-dev)
   332→- [ ] Task 1: FP8 FlashDecoding — upgrade score GEMM from bf16 to fp8 tl.dot
   333→- [ ] Task 2: FP8 value path — load fp8 KV for both score and value (halves bandwidth)
   334→- [ ] Task 3: BLOCK_N tuning — test BLOCK_N=32 to reduce KV tile VGPR pressure (kv1[32,512]=128 VGPRs)
   335→- [ ] Task 4: Threshold sweep — benchmark hybrid threshold across {50k, 100k, 200k, 500k}
   336→

   339→- [ ] Task 6: CUDA graph wrapping — wrap gemm_afp4wfp4 in CUDA graph to eliminate launch overhead
   340→- [ ] Task 7: Explore CK direct GEMM — check if Composable Kernel has a direct fp4 GEMM callable from Python

   342→### Shared

   344→
   345→**Total Tasks:** 8 | **Done:** 0 | **Left:** 8
   346→
   347→## Task Details
   348→
   349→### Task 1: MLA — FP8 Score GEMM (mla-dev)
   350→
   351→**Why:** bf16 tl.dot uses half the MFMA throughput of fp8. For compute-bound shapes (bs≤32),
   352→fp8 scoring gives ~1.5-2x speedup. The input already provides `kv_data["fp8"]`.
   353→
   354→**Approach:**
   355→1. Read `reference.py:quantize_fp8()` for Q quantization pattern
   356→2. Inside `_mla_flash_decode` kernel: load fp8 Q + fp8 KV tiles
   357→3. `qk = tl.dot(q1_fp8, tl.trans(kv1_fp8))` with fp32 accumulator
   358→4. Apply `q_scale * kv_scale * SM_SCALE` after the dot product
   359→5. Keep value accumulation in bf16 initially (Task 2 upgrades this)
   360→
   361→**Constraint:** `tl.dot` with fp8 requires BLOCK_M ≥ 16 and BLOCK_N ≥ 16 on gfx950.
   362→Current BLOCK_M=16, BLOCK_N=64 — both satisfy this.
   363→
   364→### Task 2: MLA — FP8 Value Path (mla-dev)
   365→
   366→**Why:** For bandwidth-bound shapes (bs=256, kv=8k), KV bandwidth dominates at ~288 µs (bf16).
   367→FP8 KV halves this to ~144 µs. Using fp8 for BOTH score AND value means only ONE KV load.
   368→
   369→**Approach:**
   370→1. Load fp8 KV once per tile (instead of bf16 KV for values)
   371→2. Score: `tl.dot(q_fp8, tl.trans(kv_fp8))` — already done in Task 1
   372→3. Value: `tl.dot(p.to(fp8), kv1_fp8)` — accumulate in fp32, scale at end
   373→4. Final output: `acc * kv_scale` → bf16
   374→
   375→**Risk:** FP8 value accumulation may exceed rtol=1e-2 tolerance. Mitigation: if fp8 value
   376→fails correctness, keep bf16 value path and only use fp8 for score (Task 1 alone).
   377→
   378→### Task 3: MLA — BLOCK_N Tuning (mla-dev)
   379→
   380→**Why:** The real VGPR bottleneck is `kv1[BLOCK_N, 512]`. At BLOCK_N=64: ~256 VGPRs (saturates budget).
   381→At BLOCK_N=32: ~128 VGPRs (fits with accumulator). Smaller BLOCK_N trades tile efficiency for
   382→register pressure — may eliminate spilling and improve throughput.
   383→
   384→**Approach:**
   385→1. Parameterize BLOCK_N as a tunable constexpr
   386→2. Test BLOCK_N ∈ {32, 64, 128} via benchmark
   387→3. Smaller BLOCK_N = more loop iterations but less spilling
   388→4. Larger BLOCK_N = fewer iterations but more register pressure
   389→
   390→### Task 4: MLA — Threshold Sweep (mla-dev)
   391→
   392→**Why:** Current threshold=100k was set empirically in Phase 8. With fp8 and BLOCK_N changes,
   393→the optimal threshold shifts. Need to re-benchmark.
   394→
   395→**Approach:** After Tasks 1-3, benchmark all shapes with FlashDecoding forced on vs ref_kernel.
   396→Set threshold at the crossover point.
   397→
   398→### Task 5: GEMM — Fix Fused Kernel (gemm-dev)
   399→
   400→**Why:** `submission_tritonblas_fused.py` has the Origami XCD remapping bug (non-bijective for
   401→`total_tiles % 8 != 0`). Fixing this with group-M swizzle could unlock the fused kernel path,
   402→eliminating ~10 µs of quant + launch overhead.
   403→
   404→**Approach:**
   405→1. Copy `submission_tritonblas_fused.py` → working copy
   406→2. Replace Origami XCD block with group-M swizzle (see `tritonblas-origami-xcd-remapping-bug` skill)
   407→3. Test correctness across all 5 GEMM shapes
   408→4. If passes: benchmark and compare against 20.6 µs baseline
   409→
   410→### Task 6: GEMM — CUDA Graph Wrapping (gemm-dev)
   411→
   412→**Why:** Our ~10 µs overhead per shape = A quant launch + GEMM launch + allocation.
   413→CUDA graphs capture the entire sequence and replay with ~2-3 µs overhead.
   414→
   415→**Approach:**
   416→1. Wrap `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `gemm_afp4wfp4` in a CUDA graph
   417→2. Use `torch.cuda.CUDAGraph()` with warmup + capture + replay
   418→3. Challenge: dynamic shapes require separate graphs per shape (5 shapes = 5 graphs)
   419→4. Pre-warm all 5 during first invocation
   420→
   421→**Risk:** CUDA graph capture may not work with aiter's Triton kernels (JIT compilation
   422→happens at capture time, not replay). Test empirically.
   423→
   424→### Task 7: GEMM — Explore CK Direct GEMM (gemm-dev)
   425→
   426→**Why:** If neither fused kernel nor CUDA graphs work, explore Composable Kernel (CK)
   427→for a direct fp4 GEMM path that bypasses both aiter and Triton overhead.
   428→
   429→**Approach:**
   430→1. Check if `ck` or `composable_kernel` is importable on MI355X runner
   431→2. Search for fp4/mxfp4 GEMM kernels in CK Python bindings
   432→3. If found: write a minimal submission using CK directly
   433→
   434→### Task 8: Final Submissions (Lead)
   435→
   436→Submit best results from both tracks to leaderboard.
   437→
   438→## Execution Flow (Team-Parallel)
   439→
   440→```
   441→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   442→│ Lead: TeamCreate("kernel-speedrun")                      │
   443→│ Lead: Create tasks, spawn mla-dev + gemm-dev             │
   444→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   445→│                                                          │
   446→│  mla-dev (parallel)          gemm-dev (parallel)         │
   447→│  ┌──────────────────┐       ┌──────────────────┐        │
   448→│  │ Task 1: fp8 score │       │ Task 5: fix fused │        │
   449→│  │ (read ref, write  │       │ (fix XCD, test    │        │
   450→│  │  kernel, signal)  │       │  locally, signal)  │        │
   451→│  └────────┬─────────┘       └────────┬─────────┘        │
   452→│           │                           │                   │
   453→│  Lead: submit MLA ◄──────────────────┘ Lead: submit GEMM │
   454→│  (10 min wait)                         (queued)           │
   455→│           │                                               │
   456→│  mla-dev: iterate   ◄── Lead: "MLA result: X µs"         │
   457→│  ┌──────────────────┐                                    │
   458→│  │ Task 2: fp8 value │       gemm-dev: iterate            │
   459→│  │ Task 3: BLOCK_N   │       ┌──────────────────┐        │
   460→│  └────────┬─────────┘       │ Task 6: CUDA graph │        │
   461→│           │                  │ Task 7: CK explore │        │
   462→│           ▼                  └────────┬─────────┘        │
   463→│  Lead: submit best MLA                │                   │
   464→│  Lead: submit best GEMM ◄────────────┘                   │
   465→│                                                          │
   466→│  Task 8: Final leaderboard submissions                   │
   467→│  Lead: TeamDelete("kernel-speedrun")                     │
   468→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   469→```
   470→
   471→**Efficiency gains over sequential Phase 8:**
   472→- Research + code writing happens in parallel (2 agents × ~30 min = 60 min of parallel work)
   473→- Submission pipeline stays saturated (one kernel submitting while other iterates)
   474→- Estimated: 4-6 submission cycles in ~60 min vs 2-3 sequential

   547→## Risks
   548→
   549→| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
   550→|------|--------|------------|
   551→| fp8 tl.dot on gfx950 not supported | Blocks Task 1-2 | Fall back to bf16 (current) |
   552→| fp8 value exceeds tolerance | Task 2 fails | Keep bf16 value, fp8 score only |
   553→| BLOCK_N=32 worse (more iterations) | Task 3 no improvement | Keep BLOCK_N=64 |
   554→| Fused kernel still fails after XCD fix | Task 5 fails | Keep gemm_afp4wfp4 baseline |
   555→| CUDA graph incompatible with aiter JIT | Task 6 fails | Skip, focus on fused kernel |
   556→| CK not available on runner | Task 7 fails | Accept API ceiling |
   557→| Agent coordination overhead | Slow iteration | Keep team small (3 agents) |
   558→| Edit conflicts on same file | Corruption | Strict file ownership per agent |
   559→
   560→## Verification
   561→
   562→For each kernel submission:
   563→1. `--mode test` passes (rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2)
   564→2. `--mode benchmark` improves over baseline (MLA: <129 µs, GEMM: <20.6 µs)
   565→3. `--mode leaderboard` only if benchmark shows improvement
   566→4. Lead verifies results and messages back to dev agents with performance data
