For shapes under 100k tokens, kernel launch + Python overhead dominate\n\n**Why a team approach:** Phase 8 was sequential — one agent doing MLA, then GEMM, then MoE.\nThe three kernels are independent (different files, leaderboards, and optimization strategies).\nA team can parallelize research and code writing while the lead handles sequential submissions.\n\n## Team Architecture\n\n**Team name:** `kernel-speedrun`\n\n| Agent | Role | File Ownership | Tools Needed |\n|-------|------|----------------|-------------|\n| **Lead** (main session) | Coordinates, submits via popcorn-cli, reviews results | None (reads all) | Bash (popcorn-cli), SendMessage |\n| **mla-dev** | MLA kernel research + implementation | `kernels/mixed-mla/` | Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Bash |\n| **gemm-dev** | GEMM kernel research + implementation | `kernels/mxfp4-mm/` | Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Bash |\n\n**Why 3 agents, not more:**\n- Only 2 kernels have meaningful improvement headroom (MoE is at 1.07x gap — diminishing returns)\n- Popcorn-cli submissions are sequential (~10 min each) — the lead is the bottleneck\n- More agents = more coordination overhead + linear token cost\n- 3 is the sweet spot: 2 implementers saturate the submission pipeline\n\n**Why not a dedicated research agent:**\n- Each kernel's research is highly specialized (MLA: attention algorithms; GEMM: quantized matmul)\n- A generalist researcher would duplicate context that kernel-specific agents already need\n- Each dev agent does targeted research within their domain\n\n### Communication Protocol\n\n```\nmla-dev ──writes code──→ kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py\n         ──messages────→ Lead: \"ready for submission\"\n\ngemm-dev ──writes code─→ kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py\n          ──messages───→ Lead: \"ready for submission\"\n\nLead ──submits──→ popcorn-cli (sequential, ~10 min per submit)\n     ──messages─→ mla-dev/gemm-dev: \"results: X µs, errors: Y\"\n```\n\n**Submission queue:** Lead maintains a FIFO queue. While waiting for one submission,\nthe other agent can be writing code. Typical cycle:\n1. mla-dev signals ready → Lead submits MLA → 10 min wait\n2. During wait, gemm-dev signals ready → Lead queues GEMM\n3. MLA result arrives → Lead messages mla-dev → Lead submits GEMM\n4. While GEMM runs, mla-dev iterates based on results\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n**Base path:** `.worktrees/spec-luma-amd-speedrun/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/`\n\n| File | Owner | Kernel |\n|------|-------|--------|\n| `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py` | mla-dev | MLA |\n| `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` | gemm-dev | GEMM |\n\n**Reference (read-only, shared):**\n- `kernels/mixed-mla/reference.py` — aiter fp8 MLA decode, quantize_fp8, generate_input\n- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/reference.py` — aiter gemm_a4w4 reference\n- `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_tritonblas_fused.py` — existing fused GEMM (264 lines, Origami bug)\n\n**Skills (both agents should read):**\n- `gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla` — VGPR budget, BLOCK_M bandwidth tradeoff\n- `amd-triton-jit-callsite-correctness` — gemm_a4w4 callsite sensitivity\n- `deepseek-mla-decode-flash-attention-gap` — MLA architectural analysis\n- `triton-fp4-inline-quantization` — FP4 quant patterns\n- `tritonblas-origami-xcd-remapping-bug` — Origami XCD fix\n- `popcorn-cli-amd-kernel-submission` — submission workflow\n\n## Progress Tracking\n\n### MLA Track (mla-dev)\n- [ ] Task 1: FP8 FlashDecoding — upgrade score GEMM from bf16 to fp8 tl.dot\n- [ ] Task 2: FP8 value path — load fp8 KV for both score and value (halves bandwidth)\n- [ ] Task 3: BLOCK_N tuning — test BLOCK_N=32 to reduce KV tile VGPR pressure (kv1[32,512]=128 VGPRs)\n- [ ] Task 4: Threshold sweep — benchmark hybrid threshold across {50k, 100k, 200k, 500k}\n\n### GEMM Track (gemm-dev)\n- [ ] Task 5: Investigate fused kernel fix — fix Origami XCD bug in submission_tritonblas_fused.py, test\n- [ ] Task 6: CUDA graph wrapping — wrap gemm_afp4wfp4 in CUDA graph to eliminate launch overhead\n- [ ] Task 7: Explore CK direct GEMM — check if Composable Kernel has a direct fp4 GEMM callable from Python\n\n### Shared\n- [ ] Task 8: Final leaderboard submissions — submit best versions of both kernels\n\n**Total Tasks:** 8 | **Done:** 0 | **Left:** 8\n\n## Task Details\n\n### Task 1: MLA — FP8 Score GEMM (mla-dev)\n\n**Why:** bf16 tl.dot uses half the MFMA throughput of fp8. For compute-bound shapes (bs≤32),\nfp8 scoring gives ~1.5-2x speedup. The input already provides `kv_data[\"fp8\"]`.\n\n**Approach:**\n1. Read `reference.py:quantize_fp8()` for Q quantization pattern\n2. Inside `_mla_flash_decode` kernel: load fp8 Q + fp8 KV tiles\n3. `qk = tl.dot(q1_fp8, tl.trans(kv1_fp8))` with fp32 accumulator\n4. Apply `q_scale * kv_scale * SM_SCALE` after the dot product\n5. Keep value accumulation in bf16 initially (Task 2 upgrades this)\n\n**Constraint:** `tl.dot` with fp8 requires BLOCK_M ≥ 16 and BLOCK_N ≥ 16 on gfx950.\nCurrent BLOCK_M=16, BLOCK_N=64 — both satisfy this.\n\n### Task 2: MLA — FP8 Value Path (mla-dev)\n\n**Why:** For bandwidth-bound shapes (bs=256, kv=8k), KV bandwidth dominates at ~288 µs (bf16).\nFP8 KV halves this to ~144 µs. Using fp8 for BOTH score AND value means only ONE KV load.\n\n**Approach:**\n1. Load fp8 KV once per tile (instead of bf16 KV for values)\n2. Score: `tl.dot(q_fp8, tl.trans(kv_fp8))` — already done in Task 1\n3. Value: `tl.dot(p.to(fp8), kv1_fp8)` — accumulate in fp32, scale at end\n4. Final output: `acc * kv_scale` → bf16\n\n**Risk:** FP8 value accumulation may exceed rtol=1e-2 tolerance. Mitigation: if fp8 value\nfails correctness, keep bf16 value path and only use fp8 for score (Task 1 alone).\n\n### Task 3: MLA — BLOCK_N Tuning (mla-dev)\n\n**Why:** The real VGPR bottleneck is `kv1[BLOCK_N, 512]`. At BLOCK_N=64: ~256 VGPRs (saturates budget).\nAt BLOCK_N=32: ~128 VGPRs (fits with accumulator). Smaller BLOCK_N trades tile efficiency for\nregister pressure — may eliminate spilling and improve throughput.\n\n**Approach:**\n1. Parameterize BLOCK_N as a tunable constexpr\n2. Test BLOCK_N ∈ {32, 64, 128} via benchmark\n3. Smaller BLOCK_N = more loop iterations but less spilling\n4. Larger BLOCK_N = fewer iterations but more register pressure\n\n### Task 4: MLA — Threshold Sweep (mla-dev)\n\n**Why:** Current threshold=100k was set empirically in Phase 8. With fp8 and BLOCK_N changes,\nthe optimal threshold shifts. Need to re-benchmark.\n\n**Approach:** After Tasks 1-3, benchmark all shapes with FlashDecoding forced on vs ref_kernel.\nSet threshold at the crossover point.\n\n### Task 5: GEMM — Fix Fused Kernel (gemm-dev)\n\n**Why:** `submission_tritonblas_fused.py` has the Origami XCD remapping bug (non-bijective for\n`total_tiles % 8 != 0`). Fixing this with group-M swizzle could unlock the fused kernel path,\neliminating ~10 µs of quant + launch overhead.\n\n**Approach:**\n1. Copy `submission_tritonblas_fused.py` → working copy\n2. Replace Origami XCD block with group-M swizzle (see `tritonblas-origami-xcd-remapping-bug` skill)\n3. Test correctness across all 5 GEMM shapes\n4. If passes: benchmark and compare against 20.6 µs baseline\n\n### Task 6: GEMM — CUDA Graph Wrapping (gemm-dev)\n\n**Why:** Our ~10 µs overhead per shape = A quant launch + GEMM launch + allocation.\nCUDA graphs capture the entire sequence and replay with ~2-3 µs overhead.\n\n**Approach:**\n1. Wrap `dynamic_mxfp4_quant` + `gemm_afp4wfp4` in a CUDA graph\n2. Use `torch.cuda.CUDAGraph()` with warmup + capture + replay\n3. Challenge: dynamic shapes require separate graphs per shape (5 shapes = 5 graphs)\n4. Pre-warm all 5 during first invocation\n\n**Risk:** CUDA graph capture may not work with aiter's Triton kernels (JIT compilation\nhappens at capture time, not replay). Test empirically.\n\n### Task 7: GEMM — Explore CK Direct GEMM (gemm-dev)\n\n**Why:** If neither fused kernel nor CUDA graphs work, explore Composable Kernel (CK)\nfor a direct fp4 GEMM path that bypasses both aiter and Triton overhead.\n\n**Approach:**\n1. Check if `ck` or `composable_kernel` is importable on MI355X runner\n2. Search for fp4/mxfp4 GEMM kernels in CK Python bindings\n3. If found: write a minimal submission using CK directly\n\n### Task 8: Final Submissions (Lead)\n\nSubmit best results from both tracks to leaderboard.\n\n## Execution Flow (Team-Parallel)\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│ Lead: TeamCreate(\"kernel-speedrun\")                      │\n│ Lead: Create tasks, spawn mla-dev + gemm-dev signal)  │       │  locally, signal)  │        │\n│  └────────┬─────────┘       └────────┬─────────┘        │\n│           │                           │                   │\n│  Lead: submit MLA
